# Front Cover

->[Decorative Binding]<-

<note>The volume is bound in a style typical of the 18th century, featuring a spine made of light-colored vellum or leather. The cover boards are decorated with block-printed paper, a common technique for providing an aesthetic finish to personal or library manuscripts during this period.</note>

The cover features a repeating floral lattice pattern, printed in red and green ink on a cream-colored background. This geometric and botanical motif is characteristic of decorative papers used by bookbinders to protect and embellish hand-written works.

<note>original: "No transcribable text present on this page."</note>

# Decorative Bookbinding

->[Decorative Pattern]<-

The surface of this page is covered in a repeating geometric and floral motif. It features a lattice of interlacing green lines that form a series of diamond shapes. Within each diamond, there is a stylized six-petaled flower, alternating in color between deep red and forest green. 

<note>This type of decorative paper was commonly used in the 18th and 19th centuries for book covers or endpapers. The pattern is created using woodblock printing or stenciling techniques, providing an aesthetic finish to the manuscript's exterior.</note>

<summary>This page displays a well-preserved example of historical decorative paper used in bookbinding, featuring a repeating green lattice and floral motif. It contains no written text but serves as an important artifact of book history and ornamental design.</summary>

<keywords>bookbinding, decorative paper, floral pattern, geometric motif, endpaper, print culture</keywords>

<margin>Fore-edge decoration</margin>
<note>The fore-edge is the edge of the book's pages opposite the spine. In historical bookbinding, these edges were often painted or "gauffered" (indented with heated tools) to show the owner's wealth or the book's importance.</note>

->**Visual Description of the Volume**<-

The image displays the decorated fore-edge of a closed volume. It features a repeating geometric and floral pattern applied with green and orange-red pigments. This type of decoration was common in early modern bookbinding to protect the paper and provide aesthetic appeal when the book was stored on a shelf with the edges facing outward.

# Antonius Laurus of the Palaces, Paduan Doctor of Civil Law

->Doctor, whose least title is Imperial Count Palatine, etc. To the Hairy and the Bald <note>original: "Capillate & Calue" - likely a playful or proverbial address to all men, young and old</note>, having experienced the Remedies of Both Fortunes, both prosperous and adverse circumstances and strengths: He prays for happy successes for the Good according to their vows.<-

---

**W**hen from my tender—as the Greeks say—little nails <note>original: "Unguiculis" - a Latin idiom meaning 'from earliest childhood'</note>, beginning to devote myself to the literary studies of Pallas <note>The goddess of wisdom</note>, I recount in my mind: by Hercules, I do not open a book—as I now perceive—which does not carry upon its face a clear antonomasia <note>A figure of speech where an epithet or title replaces a proper name</note> of this most admirable and nearly divine Astronomy, the Princess and Queen of all liberal arts. And if anyone is perhaps ignorant, incapable, or incredulous of the sense and understanding of such a kernel—as often happens—letting pass Solon and other Greek sages, and the philosophers, Ptolemy and all the astronomers: if in the cause itself the judges or witnesses are suspected by the single law <note>original: "L. Ne quis in sua causa" - a legal principle that no one should be a judge in their own cause</note> regarding witnesses. How can he know, or be able, or dare to read and understand the first documents of grammar, or likewise P. Clergerius, Tully <note>Cicero</note>, Macrobius, Pliny, and the choir of poets? Then, gradually ascending to the first cradles of the Laws, did not our legislator Caesar Justinian command the sacred *Institutions* to be divided into four books in that same proem? Because just as all bodies are composed from the strengths of the four elements—namely earth, water, air, and fire (for earth corresponds to melancholy, water to phlegm, air to blood, and fire to red choler)—universally embracing the four humors of man, just as the year is wonderfully divided into the four seasons: which are spring, summer, autumn, and winter. Furthermore, did not our aforementioned Legist limit the time of this brief life of ours in the laws <note>original: "l. h. c. de sa. san. eccle." - referring to the Code of Justinian</note> there, unless they have exceeded a cycle of one hundred years? For that is the end of a long-lived man's life. And in the *Authentica* <note>A collection of Justinian's Novels</note> regarding the Roman Church, it is recognized that this time is often the end of a long-lived man. And in the *Authentica* concerning Monks, it says: "This which John, a man most famous in his way of life and beloved of God, recently announced to us from Lycia, being nearly in the one hundred and twentieth year of his age, yet strong in the virtues of the soul and the operations of the body," etc. And in the *Authentica* regarding things not to be alienated: "For what will be stable among men and so immobile that it suffers no change, when our entire state consists under perpetual motion?" Moreover, did not Paul of Padua, Prince of the Law-Givers, and Accursius the Florentine glossator, Bartolus and Baldus, and all the consultants of the Law, most prudently determine by their opinion at what hour we ought to begin the day? And to comprehend many things in few words: in the laws concerning Hermaphrodites, the seventh month, those who are not children, and Arethusa, regarding the status of men; do not Arithmetic, Music, and Astronomy contain within themselves the truth? For if anyone unskilled in Mathematics wishes to write against the Mathematicians, he will be open to laughter. If, therefore, the Monarchs of both Laws <note>Civil and Canon Law</note> have most prudently met in one opinion with the philosophers, physicians, and astronomers, what shall we say to these things, except: "The uncertain crowd is split into contrary pursuits" <note>A quote from Virgil's Aeneid</note>. How then shall a priest be able to announce the Moveable Feasts that preserve our Nazarene faith—varying by the course of the Moon—without any lunar observation? How can a sailor observe the North or South Pole, the regions of the world, the increase and decrease of the waters, give sails to the winds, or plow the seas, avoiding adverse waves and reaching the ports of safety without consideration of the heavens? How shall the farmer till the earth by sowing, planting, grafting, cutting, and gathering the fruits, according to that passage in the *Georgics*: "What makes the crops joyous, under what star it is fitting to turn the earth, Maecenas, and to join the vines to the elms; what care for the oxen, what cultivation for the flock, and how much experience for the thrifty bees—hence I shall begin to sing. O you brightest lights of the world, who lead the gliding year through the sky! Father Bacchus and nourishing Ceres," etc. Likewise, the most prudent physician, healing the bodies of the sick, healthfully forbids the suffering to receive bloodletting or pharmacy on the days of conjunctions and oppositions, or during the "dog days" <note>The hot days of summer associated with Sirius, the Dog Star</note>. Since, by the testimony of his own Hippocrates, Astronomy is no small part of medicine. And therefore it is necessary for Doctors to have the science of astronomy; whence doctors might administer medicine under a certain constellation which quickly kills, which yet in another could heal. Indeed, the cautious Surgeon will avoid touching a limb of the Microcosm <note>The human body</note> with a cautery or iron while the Moon is in the sign of that limb. For that would be to wound them and, by multiplying the inconvenience, to add affliction to the afflicted. How shall Princes be able to fortify citadels, foresee wars, and avoid the evil of epidemic and the penury of victuals, food, and drink—as the most wise Solon advised—unless they were made more certain through the annual prognostication of the Astronomers? Therefore, with greatest effort and cheerful study, receive our immense labors and midnight vigils contained briefly in this golden Little Work; how much Michael Scot and Guido Bonatti of Forlì and others magnify this, there is no one who does not know, both by their brief style and by the very clear experience of things and the greatest truth of many of their judgments. As I have often—I speak among the learned—seen the proof. Let, therefore, *Albubather* <note>A reference to the 9th-century Persian astrologer Abu Bakr al-Hasan ibn al-Khasib</note> be in the eyes of your Excellencies as delightful as a unique phoenix. According to the Satirist: "A rare bird on earth, and very much like a black swan." And if I see that this is to be deleted in your estimation, I shall take care to exhibit tomorrow another thing, also rare and distinguished, according to the strength of my narrow fortune and my talent, for the common utility, exercise, health, and cheerfulness of students.

<summary>Antonius Laurus provides a scholarly defense of astronomy, arguing that it is an essential foundation for law, medicine, agriculture, and navigation. He cites numerous legal authorities, such as Justinian and Accursius, alongside classical poets and physicians to justify the study of the stars.</summary>
<keywords>Astronomy, Civil Law, Justinian, Medicine, Astrology, Four Elements, Four Humors, Michael Scot, Guido Bonatti, Albubather, Legal Theory, Navigation</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page begins the primary text of Albubather's astrological treatise, following the introductory frontispiece. It establishes the foundational methodology for casting a nativity (birth chart).</meta>

<page-num>2</page-num>
<sig>A 2</sig>

# The Book of the Great Albubather, Son of Al-Khasili Al-Kharis
# Distinguished Author of Astronomy.

<margin>Chapter 1</margin>
**T**he first thing that must be done in the work of a nativity <note>A birth chart or horoscope</note> is to take the degree of the Ascendant and its minute, as well as the degrees of the other Houses which signify the matters of the Native <note>The person for whom the chart is cast</note>, such as his: <margin>House 1</margin> **Life**. <margin>House 2</margin> ¶ **Money**. <margin>House 3</margin> ¶ **Brothers**. <margin>House 4</margin> ¶ **Parents**. <margin>House 5</margin> ¶ **Children**. <margin>House 6</margin> ¶ **Sicknesses**. <margin>House 7</margin> ¶ **Marriages**. <margin>House 8</margin> ¶ **Death**. <margin>House 9</margin> ¶ **Journeys**. <margin>House 10</margin> ¶ **Kings** <note>Career or social status</note>. <margin>House 11</margin> ¶ **Friends** and <margin>House 12</margin> ¶ **Enemies**.

¶ You must also verify the Planets: you should know their movements—namely their Longitude and Latitude, their Oriental or Occidental positions, their Stations, Retrogradations, and also their Ascensions and Descensions. You must know which of them are Strong or Weak, Light or Heavy, Superior or Inferior, Fortunate or Unfortunate, and their Joys. Also, which are Good in themselves or Evil, whether by nature or by half-nature. Furthermore, you must know their Binding and Separation, the <term>Micheliz Alieber</term> <note>A technical term likely referring to planetary strength or "emptiness of course"</note>, their Changes, Returns, Receptions, and the Conjunctions of their Lights. One must know the Aspect of one to another: namely the Left and Right, the Trine, Sextile, Square, and Opposition, as well as the Projections of the Rays of the Houses, the Exaltations, Terms, Faces, and Triplicities, and their <term>Algebu tar</term> <note>original: "Algebu tar"; likely a corruption of the Arabic 'al-jabr', referring to planetary calculations or adjustments</note>. Also their Addition and Diminution, Friendship, Enmity, and Eclipse. And which one is adding or subtracting in the calculation and their complexion.

¶ Moreover, you must know the Signs: Fixed, Movable, and Common; those that Ascend Directly or Tortuously; and which are Fiery, Airy, Watery, or Earthy. Also their Colors and Forms, their Oriental and Occidental nature. And which are Southern or Northern, Vernal, Estival <note>Summer</note>, Autumnal, or Hibernal <note>Winter</note>. Also their Tastes and Smells, their Bright and Dark degrees, Empty and Smoky degrees, and the degrees of Shadow or the Shadowy ones, Windy and Non-windy. Also the <term>Azemena</term> <note>Degrees associated with physical defects or chronic illness</note> and Pitted degrees which give Chronic Sicknesses. ¶ Afterward, you must know all the Modes of Signification and the parts of the <term>Beibenia</term> <note>original: "Beibenia"; refers to the fixed stars of the first or second magnitude</note>, that is, the Fixed Stars of the first or second magnitude. Also the consideration and state of the Moon, such as her Additions and Diminutions, her Journeys, her Equality and Inequality, and her adaptation: her voidness, cutting off, and appearance, and her place in the boundaries of the signs and terms of both the Fortunes and Infortunes. Also her projection or aspects to the fortunes or infortunes. The delay of the birth, and also for 40 days before or after the Birth, and her conjunction and opposition with the Sun. ¶ You must also know the form and genus of the native: whether the Birth is of a Human or of a high Animal, or a Bird, or a Fish. ¶ Then follows the Order of the aforesaid 12 Houses. ¶ Look also at the delay of the falling of the seed <margin>House 1</margin> in the Womb, and the delay of the one born in the Mother's belly. And whether the birth will be swift or slow, and if he is scarcely born as an abortion or dies at that very hour, or whether he may live long or have a short life. Also the manner of the native in his nature and Complexion: his faithfulness and unfaithfulness. Know also his foolishness, Beauty or ugliness, Obedience and disobedience, his Slowness in work and his speed, Boldness and fear. His Strength in <unclear>summons</unclear> and lightness. Cheerfulness, anger, and sadness. His disposition in his heart regarding those things said of him, and in his disposition: Lying and truth, Strength and weakness, Generosity and greed, Humility and pride, Shame and shamelessness, Fortune and Misfortune. ¶ And through the aforesaid observations you will be able to know: whether the native will be rich or poor, and by what means he might gain or lose, and of what mastery <note>profession</note> he will be. And how he will behave in his childhood. And if he who nourished him is his father or not. ¶ You will also be able to know the state of his brothers: both the older, middle, and younger, and also the state of his sisters: or how many brothers or Sisters there will be, and which of them will die before or after him. Also the state of friendship and enmity, and their mutual gain or loss. And if the things of the native will have their inheritance, or he himself their inheritances. And which of the brothers will be Rich or poor.

¶ You will also be able to know the Parentage of the native's parents, whether they be of a Small or great lineage; whether they be of one status or diverse, and which of them is greater than the other, namely the Father or the mother. And if the native is loved by them or not; and if they will nourish him or put him on his way. Also which of the parents loves him more, and to which of them he will be more similar: namely the Father or Mother, or the Brothers. And if the father will be sick with a Curable or incurable sickness, and likewise the mother, and which dies first. And by what sickness their death will be. And if the native will have inheritances or not and when. Also what the parents will have of gain or labor from the native, and whether the native will remain with them or be separated from them. ¶ You will also be able to know whether the native is fit to have children or not; and if fit, whether he will have daughters, and at what time. Also how many will be Males or females; whether they will live or not; whether they will prosper or not, and which of them will be elevated or depressed. And if the Males will be stronger or weaker than themselves. Also which of the children will be fortunate or unfortunate, Free or servants, and if they die before him or he before them. ¶ You can also know the state of the native's sickness, and where his sicknesses will be, and at what time, and whether the sickness will be in the body or the spirit, and in which member. ¶ You will also be able to know the Marriage of the native: whether he will have a wife or not. If he ought to have a wife, how he will receive her, and whether she will be of a greater lineage or lesser than himself. Whether she will be a girl <note>virgin</note> or not, Small or great, Fickle or of the common people, Free or a handmaid. And if she will be fortunate or unfortunate with him, or if he will pursue gain or loss with her. Or if she will be an adulteress or not. If she will love him or not. If the native will be in the power of the wife or the wife in the power of the native. If the native will be jealous of the wife or trust in her; if he will care much for her or not. If the wife will be healthy or sick, and how many wives he will have. From which one he will have children, and also whether he will die before the wife or not. ¶ You will also be able to know well how the death of the native will be. If he will be killed by another or not; if he will die by himself naturally, or by a shameful death. If he dies in his own Land or in a foreign land. ¶ You will also be able to know his Journeys, and if he will travel or not. If the journeys will be by Land or by Sea, and if they will prosper or not, and at what time they will happen. Also whether he will do them openly or secretly, and for what causes, and if he will return from them to his own place or not. ¶ You will also be able to know the native's mastery and his lordship, whether he will have it from a King or not. Or if he will acquire loss or gain from the King, and how it will be with his King. Whether he will be powerful or not. If he dies in the Kingdom or outside the Kingdom. If he will acquire any Kingdom or House in his own Land or in a foreign one. And how he will obtain that Kingdom or Lordship. ¶ You will also be able to know the state of the native's Friends: whether they will be powerful or poor, or if he will lack friends, or if his friends will need him or he them. And if they will love him or not. If men will cherish his friendships or despise them. And if his friends will follow gain or loss from him, or he from them. ¶ You will also be able to know the state of his Enemies, and their multitude or fewness, their strength and weakness. And if he will incur loss from them or not, or if they will be strangers or kin. And let these things be said concerning the 12 houses. ¶ Through the <term>Hyleg</term> <note>The "Giver of Life," a specific point in the chart used to determine longevity</note> and the <term>Alcocoden</term> <note>The "Giver of Years," a planet that determines the length of life</note> and the planets themselves looking upon them, you will also be able to know the state and quantity of the native's Life by the Will of God. ¶ Through the lords of the Triplicity, you will be able to know the good or evil to come to the native in the 1st, 2nd, and 3rd parts of life. ¶ Through the lord of the term, you will be able to know the native's fortune and labor. ¶ Through the <term>Athazir</term> <note>original: "athazir"; a method of planetary progression or direction used to time events</note> of the Hyleg to the aspects of the fortunes and infortunes, you will know the times in which good or evil will happen to the native, and how, and whether or not. ¶ Through the <term>Athazir</term> of the Sun, you will know his sublimity or depression, and the state of his presence and of greater honors. Also of the kingdom and lordship, if he should have a kingdom or lordship. ¶ Through the <term>Athazir</term> of the Moon, you will be able to know the sickness which he will have in body or in soul and his <unclear>testimony</unclear>. ¶ Through the <term>Athazir</term> of the Part of Fortune, you will know his fortune, height, and elevation, and how he will behave in this. ¶ Through the <term>Athazir</term> of the degree of the Ascendant, you will know the state of his Life and his pilgrimage, and how he will behave in his land. ¶ Through the <term>Athazir</term> of the 10th house, you will know the works and masteries of the native and also the honor he will have of good or evil, beauty and deformity. ¶ Through the <term>Athazir</term> of Saturn, you will know the explanation of the native's affairs; you will know the journeys of friends and the affairs of grandfathers, fathers, and older brothers, and the state of their inheritance. ¶ Through the <term>Athazir</term> of Jupiter, you will know the friendship which the native will have with Kings or powerful people, and whatever will be of form and beauty and also of character. ¶ Through...

<summary>This page outlines the comprehensive scope of Albubather's astrological system, detailing how the twelve houses and various planetary calculations (like Hyleg and Athazir) predict every aspect of a person's life, from health and wealth to the manner of their death.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, nativity, twelve houses, Hyleg, Alcocoden, Athazir, planetary aspects, prediction, medieval science</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological influences on human life, moving from general social relations to the specific embryological process of conception and gestation governed by the planets.</meta>

Through the *athazir* <note>original: "athazir"; an Arabic astrological term referring to the "direction" or progression of a planet</note> of Mars, you shall know his fornications, marriages, loves, friendships, and the loss or profit from women, as well as disputes and oppositions. Through the *athazir* of Venus, you shall know love and marriages, sexual intercourse and delights, the goodness of the soul, and joy with women and girls. Along with the aforementioned, it is also necessary to know the location of the Dragon’s Head and its Tail <note>The lunar nodes: the North Node (Head) and South Node (Tail)</note>, and all the planets with them. When you have carefully investigated what I have said and have properly cast the figure of the heavens, judge every birth according to the judgments handed down in this book, in which I have explained and collected to the best of my ability the sayings of all the wise men who were before me. Know, however, that a wise man, through his own wisdom and subtlety, will be able to easily lead the judgments concerning births declared in this volume toward the truth and the true trinity of science.

### On the Perfection of the Seed in the Womb. Chapter 1.

**T**he first wise men, who were of sound intellect and experienced in the proofs of the significations of the stars, said that the casting of the seed <note>conception</note> holds a likeness to the sign and state of the newborn above all significations extracted from the Ascendant when the newborn emerges from the mother's womb. For the casting of the seed into the womb is like the heat of a fire which exceeds all the warmth of baths; it is like the heart in a bow, the iron in a spear, the sword in the hand, the weight in a wheel, and the root from which the said things are extracted: the foundation. Therefore, when you know the hour and day of the casting of the seed into the womb, and the locations of the planets in the minds <note>or "signs"</note> that signify that seed, then you will be secure in that upon which you can found and firm up your judgment. For if you are ignorant of this, you will perhaps be deceived. I wish, therefore, to hand down the immediate fall of the sperm from the day it fell until the day it emerges from the mother's womb, and the quantity of its delay in the womb, and the order of the planets with it, by the will of God. Thus the wise men have said and recorded in their books: Know that the matter of the seed will be according to the likeness of the foods received in the body. And the root of that matter is coldness and moisture. But when the natural color <note>or "heat"</note> works in the womb, it makes it hot and moist. Indeed, heat and moisture existing in any member of the body causes the matter to descend from every place of the body through the motion of intercourse. From every place of the body it descends and becomes white, like the milk existing in the breasts, which is transmitted from various vapors in the form of blood to the breasts and is converted into whiteness through the cooking of the natural color. And so it is made through agitation and natural heat. First, therefore, the matter of the seed descends from the brain, which is white, cold, and moist. Second, it comes principally from the liver, which is hot and moist and the mine of blood. Third and subsequently, it comes from the other members until it reaches the testicles; and the testicles give it to the members, and the member to the womb. And the wise men say that the womb has many follicles, or places in which that seed can be divided. And a woman, according to the quantity of places in which the matter is divided, can have many sons or daughters; it has the quantity of matter in the said follicles, which are seven receptacles. For if the seed descends to only one follicle of the womb, she will have a single son or daughter; if to two, two; and so on up to seven. Now, when the seed has fallen into the womb, the menstrual blood will nourish it and will be the mother of that seed; and then it joins with it and ceases for the women until the fetus emerges from the womb. When the seed first falls into the womb, Saturn will dispose it in the first month, because he is higher than the other planets and his circle is superior. The seed will therefore be, according to its nature, cold and congealed in that month, and it has this quantity of coldness and dryness from Saturn. And because the nature of Saturn is closer and more disposed to death than to life, therefore the seed of the man in that month will not be mixed with the seed of the woman, but each will be separate, like the white of an egg from the yolk; and so it will remain from the day the seed fell into the womb until the first month has passed. Afterward, Jupiter will dispose that seed, who is the planet of wind and change; and the wind of Jupiter will move it, by the will of God, until both seeds are mixed together and made of one nature. And then that seed will be mobile, from whose motion in the vessel of the woman there will happen to the woman nausea and a change of soul. So much so that the whole body will change beyond measure by the change of the wind and the seed in the womb. A newborn, therefore, who has much of such wind will be wise, because that wind is more subtle than the soul; and it is mixed with the body and exits from it before the soul; and that wind has its seat in the body and the soul in the brain. For from that wind proceed the thoughts of the newborn, just as from the soul proceed the five senses. Likewise, therefore, the wind directs the natural heat. So much so that when a man dies, the heat of that wind remains in the heart of the man and afterward recedes; from whose recession the whole body afterward grows cold and dries up. Certain wise men have said that when in the second month both

seeds—namely, of the man and the woman—are mixed together by the power of that wind, the person will be a sleeper who always sleeps; so much so that such motion cannot be called life or death. But with this, it is added with that wind: which wind in the fourth month will mix the soul with the seed and will remain in the body with the soul. When the soul has dominion over that wind, it will bring forth life. When the soul is dominated by the wind, it will flow into a nature of sleepiness. And that seed will be of diverse mutation according to which one has dominion over the other. For when one dominates over the other, its effect will appear, and the affection of the other will somewhat flow away. Know also that when the Sun has dominion over the Moon through conjunction, the light of the Moon will not have vigor because of the light of the Sun. The wind of Jupiter will not move from the seed until the third month comes, which then is disposed by Mars, because the circle of Mars follows the circle of Jupiter. And because Mars is the planet of blood, he will change that seed into his own nature and heat, by the will of God. Therefore, that seed will be coagulated in the manner of blood, and the wind of Jupiter will hide itself and grow cold; that which was moved from the body by that wind, such as the nausea which the woman had in the second month, will cease. Therefore, that seed will remain in the disposition of Mars until the fourth month comes. And then it will be in the disposition of the Sun, who by his dryness will remove its superfluous moisture. And it will return as a mixed thing, in which God will give strength and a soul; and He will fulfill His work in it; and it will appear in it and receive the strength and form of a man. Afterward, in the fifth month, the disposition returns, by the will of God, to Venus, who forms the shape of the members and their uses will appear; then hair will grow on it and it will receive senses. Then the disposition of the sixth month returns to Mercury, who is the planet of vocalization and sense; then, therefore, its tongue and its members will move so that they stretch out, pressing into the womb with hands and feet; and its motion will be strengthened. So much so that it does not desire to remain in such a narrow place. But it busies itself seeking an exit from the darkness of the womb to the world with all its strength. In the seventh month, however, its disposition comes to the Moon; and because its form and the strength of its members are completed, if it is born in that month, it will be complete and fit for life. But if it perseveres in the womb until the eighth month, its disposition returns a second time to Saturn, by whom, according to his nature, it will be chilled and congealed, and coldness will remain in it through that whole month; whence if any is born in that month, he will be cold and dry and poorly suited for life. And frequently many are born dead in that month, or die immediately. Afterward, in the ninth month, its disposition returns again to Jupiter; those, therefore, who are born in that month are commonly disposed to life because of the nature of Jupiter. For which reason it should be known that those who are born in the months attributed to Saturn, Mars, the Sun, and Mercury die quickly or emerge dead, especially if the aforementioned four planets were unfortunate. Therefore, the progression toward a life disposed for living will be in the months attributed to Jupiter, Venus, and the Moon.

### On the Delay of the Newborn in the Mother's Womb. Chapter 2.

**C**ertain men have said that the delay itself will be according to the quantity of the sign in which the Sun is, to the ninth sign, according to the space between its House and its Exaltation. As from Leo, which is the House of the Sun, to Aries, in which the Sun is exalted, there are nine signs. Give, therefore, to each sign, according to the said manner, one month. And so it will be seen that the Sun signifies the affairs of the newborn and his hour, as well as the ascending degree, the Angles, the houses, and his creation. Through him, therefore, you shall know the state of the newborn, and his years, and the times of loss and gain. Therefore, when the seed has fallen into the womb of the woman and the Sun is in any of the signs, he will be the significator of that from the hour of the fall of the seed in the womb until the hour of the progression of the newborn from the mother's womb; and the Sun will not move from that signification in the sign and its mutation until he has exited the mother's womb. Therefore, the Sun leads him from mode to mode until he reaches the fifth sign from his place in which he was when he began; because in the space of eight signs he fulfills the four natures, namely: Fire, Water, Air, and Earth. And then the Sun returns to the nature of the sign in which he began. And this happens in the fifth month from the casting of the seed, in which the form of the newborn is completed and its members take on flesh. Sight opens and it begins to be in the form of its parents. Therefore, the fifth sign, because of its nature and the similarity it has with the Ascendant, signifies children. For when the Ascendant is an Earthy sign, it will be Earthy. And if it is Fiery, it will be Fiery. If Watery, Watery. If Airy, Airy. And when the Sun enters the ninth sign and completes the triplicity of heaven and fulfills its nature, its mutations in the signs with its elevation, descent, addition, and diminution will be completed.

its form and building: It will appear and be born by the will of God.
When the child is born complete, it has the hands and face of the mother above its knees, and is joined by the mother's umbilical cord; it flees the food from which it is nourished. If it is a male, its face will be turned toward the mother's kidneys. If it is a female, its face will be turned toward the mother's belly. Therefore, when a male emerges, he will have his face raised toward Heaven; a female, however, toward the earth. When, therefore, it exits from the darkness of the womb, it is because of the squeezing it suffers and the pushing of the wind existing in the mother's belly; it sneezes and emits cries of pain. And afterward, if the birth was by day, it raises its eyes to the light of the sun. If by night, to the light of a candle or another light. And it marvels at the state in which it is and that in which it was before.
God also provided that it should have care for itself and fear in its heart: And we see this through experience. When we lift little ones from the earth into the air, they always hold onto us or our clothes; if they cannot do this, they emit cries. And this is the first thing that God placed in its heart and in the Brain: And also the right-handed intellect <note>original: "intellectū diextu vnutē", likely referring to instinctive coordination or natural faculty</note> by which the infant seeks to be suckled by milk and to suck the breasts; and also to weep because of one who offends it and to be quiet with the one who nourishes it. Therefore, let God be glorified, who by His wisdom does such wonders which man cannot attain. Blessed also be His name. For He Himself gives life and death when He wills.

### Concerning the Knowledge of the Animodar <note>A technical term in astrology for a method used to rectify a birth chart to find the exact degree of the Ascendant.</note> and the Hour of the Projection of the Seed into the Womb. Chapter 3.

When you wish to know the Ascending Degree and its Minute: the hour of birth: and the stay of the newborn in the mother's womb, through this direct the ascending degree and the planets. Afterward, look at the Moon: if it is between the ascending degree and the descending degree under the earth. Take the degrees existing from the descending degree to the degree of the Moon and double that: Then divide the doubled amount by 24. And what remains after the division will be hours: And what appears in the quotient will be days. These you ought to add to the 273 days, by which the average stay in the womb is signified: and what results will be the stay of the newborn in the mother's womb. If, however, you find the Moon between the ascending degree and the descending degree above the earth: Take the degrees which are from the descending degree to the degree of the Moon and double them. Then divide the doubled amount by 24: And what comes out will be days, and what remains will be hours: These days and hours you ought to add to the shorter stay, which is 258 days. And what results will be the stay of the newborn in the mother's womb. From which it appears that the average stay is greater than the shorter stay by 15 days. If you add these to the average stay, the longer stay will result, which contains 282 days. If, therefore, you find the Moon in the degree of the East <note>the Ascendant</note> or the West <note>the Descendant</note>: The stay in the mother's womb will be 288 days.

### Concerning the Same.

Direct the ascendant according to your ability as you wish. Then take the degrees which are from the descending degree to the degree of the moon: join these with the degrees of the equation placed in the table of the direct circle and divide the sum by 12: And what remains will be the mean motion of the sun: to which you ought to add 258. And what results after the addition will be the stay of the newborn in the mother's womb. Then extract from the mean motion of the sun held at the hour of exit from the mother's womb its motion during the stay of the days of the newborn in the mother's womb, and what remains will be the mean motion of the sun. Afterward, verify that mean motion and where you find the sun: look at how many hours of the day there are. Then multiply those by the time of the hours: Afterward, you will find the ascending degree: To which the degree of the Moon should agree at birth: and then see what the degree of the Moon will be then. And then project from the degree of that ascendant. And that which comes out from the multiplication of the hours: Return to equal hours: which you will add with the mean motion of the Moon in the hour in which the sun begins to ascend on that day. Afterward, direct the place of the moon in its sign: And know that the newborn whose stay in the mother's womb was from the average stay to the longer stay will have in its body some likeness which will be added: In some member of its body. And the newborn whose stay was toward the shorter stay will have in some member of its body a diminution or defect.

### Concerning the Same.

Another way concerning the stay of the newborn in the mother's womb. Direct the Moon at the hour of birth and know its place. Then direct it for one whole year after birth: And for one year before birth: Which being done, consider the three aforesaid places: and if they look upon each other in a trine aspect <note>an astrological aspect of 120 degrees, considered harmonious</note>: The stay of the newborn in the mother's womb was 9 months: and then the newborn shall live, by the will of God. If, however, it does not look upon itself, the newborn stayed in the womb for eight months. If two of the aforesaid places do not look upon each other, the stay of the newborn in the womb was 7 months. When, therefore, you have the hour of the falling of the seed into the womb and the stay of the newborn in the womb: direct the planets and their places, beginning the months from the hour in which the seed fell into the womb. These things having been explained, let us pass to the significance of the months according to the order of the planets: And we have already said that Saturn has the disposition of the first month from the falling of the seed into the womb.

When Saturn is strong in that month, and in its own house, exaltation, triplicity, term, or face <note>these are technical "essential dignities" in astrology that measure a planet's strength</note>: That seed will be light, and it will free the pregnant woman from pain and sickness harming her. And that seed will be raised to the upper parts of the belly, and her breath will be quiet and her manners beautiful. And if Saturn is in the term of Jupiter, the pregnant woman will rejoice in her pregnancy, having good thoughts and confidence in God, because He will deliver her; she will be healthy in body and of good breath, hoping that the end of that pregnancy will be good. If it is in the term of Mars: many heats and sicknesses will invade her, and she will have many changes in her body; and she will see menstrual blood in her pregnancy and have pains in the inner part of the belly, and her pregnancy will be on the right side. If it is in the term of Venus: she will be glad, and she will have the seed safe in that pregnancy, with strength and health: and her color will be beautiful, and beauty will appear in her face. If in the term or house of Mercury: she will be light in that pregnancy and rejoicing, with much change and little rest: as long as that pregnancy lasts, she will recount the months and days of her pregnancy and birth. If Saturn is in its descent <note>debilitated</note>, diminished in number in its house or term, the breath of the pregnant woman will be narrow: and she will have distress in her chest, and in that pregnancy thought and pain with little joy: and her soul will not be quiet: but she will fear much for her soul and have bad dreams, and see fearful things while sleeping and waking: And she will be daily thinking of death: and she will weigh her birth heavily: pain will descend to the inner parts of the belly: neither fear nor weeping will be removed from her until after the birth. And if Saturn, being in the same disposition, is in the term of Mars: the pregnant woman will have hot and moist and bloody pains: but she will be rejoicing and of much movement. And if Saturn, thus disposed, is in the house or term of Jupiter: the pregnant woman will suffer a windy and hot sickness. But the end of her birth will be healthy and strong then. And if Saturn, thus disposed, is in the term of Venus: the joy of the pregnant woman will be diminished: and she would wish that she were not pregnant: yet her end will be good: and her birth healthy. And if Saturn, thus disposed, is in the term of Mars or with the Tail of the Dragon <note>the South Node of the Moon, usually considered malefic</note>: Fear will invade the pregnancy, so that the fetus may perish in the womb: unless God should wish to preserve it.

Jupiter has the disposition of the 2nd month from the falling of the seed into the womb, and he signifies faith, sense, intellect, and knowledge. When, therefore, in the second month he is strong and in his ascension (that is, ascending in his circle) and increasing in number: he will dispose the sense of the newborn and its simplicity, intellect, and wisdom: and according to his ascension and elevation, he will dispose in it wisdom and faith in its words: and will give it knowledge which it has not heard, nor has anyone taught you. And if with this he is in his apogee: it signifies that he will be a reciter of things which others do not know: and he will place roots in them from himself: he will speak with providence: and then he will be reputed a prophet. And if he is in his own house, it signifies that the newborn will be wise in the Books of faith. And if he is in his exaltation, the newborn will provoke men to faith: He will teach them the wonders of the saints: Especially if he is in Sagittarius. And if Jupiter is in the house or term of Mars: the newborn will destroy men and provoke them to killings and wars. And if he is in the house or term of Venus: The newborn will be a speaker and preacher: he will be beautifully speaking and a singer: So that by his singing and words he will provoke men to tears. And if he is in the house or term of Mercury: The newborn will attract men to himself by the pleasantness of his words and their sagacity. And if he is in the house or term of Saturn, he will be an experimenter: and will seek to do wonders upon men and show them marvels by lies, which is his art. And if Jupiter is in his descent: it signifies the detriment of the newborn and the smallness of its intellect; nor does it do or say anything except what it has heard and seen from others. And this will be according to its descent...

# Mars has the governance of the third month from the casting of the seed into the womb. And it is the planet of audacity, strength, and heat. When in the third month Mars is in its ascension, it signifies audacity and the fulfillment of deeds with boldness. The child will be strong, prone to anger, and light of body. He will seek out disputes and provoke them quickly without fear; nor will he repent for anything he has done. And if, in addition to this, Mars is in its apogee, the child will be an initiator of wars and battles, and a sower of discord after peace has been made—especially if Mars is in its own house or term, and in Scorpio. But if, with Mars thus disposed, the birth is nocturnal, the child will be a master of wars and movements, and bold in all things; nor will he be able to endure injuries. And if the birth is diurnal, the child will be wrathful, great of heart, a shedder of blood, of little mercy, of a harsh will, high-minded, and boasting excessively of himself. And if Mars is in the house or term of Jupiter, it signifies that the child's audacity and strength will be tempered with sense, and he will act with deliberation and knowledge, even avoiding wars, treasons, and things that are without cause. And if Mars is in the house or term of Venus, the child will be exceedingly jealous, and by occasion of his jealousy, he will come to killings and other evil deeds. And if it is in the house of Mercury, the child will strike fear into others with haste and malice. And if it is in the house of Saturn, he will be wrathful, heavy of heart, and prone to evil, weighty in wars and killings. But if Mars is in its descent, the child will be fearful, weak of heart, fearing wars and the shedding of blood. And if in all these things it is in the opposition of its apogee, he will be a mocker of all traditions, like a woman, and enduring injuries beyond measure. But if, with this, Mars is in its own house or term with the Dragon's Tail <note>The South Node of the Moon, usually considered a malefic influence</note>, the child will be a fool and will throw stones at men; he will have an evil and shameful nickname, and thinking on this, he will quickly be provoked to anger and will throw stones at them.

# The Sun has governance over the fourth month from the casting of the seed into the womb. And it is the planet of height, sublimity, beauty, strength, and victory above all other planets. Therefore, when in the fourth month the Sun is in its own house, or its exaltation, triplicity, or face, or if other planets receive it, it signifies the completion of the limbs and their perfection, beauty, virtue, and the growth of the body. The child will be strong of body and in all limbs. By the virtue of his strength, he will quickly be able to conquer wild beasts and destroy them, and break hard stones. And if the Sun is in the house or term of Mars, the child will have audacity along with his strength and perfection in all his limbs; he will be magnanimous, by reason of which he will wish to be believed wealthy, and without cause he will take things from men and will scarcely have enough for his own affairs. And if the Sun is in the house or term of Mercury, or is pushed and received by him, the child will be wise and joyful, and his strength will be joined with art, genius, and knowledge—especially if the Sun is in Gemini, because there it is greatly strengthened by its ascent. It will thus dispose the child in the formation of his limbs and the completion of his body, so that by the strength of his hands he will quickly accomplish things. And if the Sun is in the strength of Venus, especially in Taurus, the child will be beautiful, complete, corpulent, strong, astute, and wise; and his strength will be in his neck and shoulders. By his virtues, he will quickly be able to lift and sustain as much weight as three or four men can carry; yet he will be lazy, heavy, and of dull intellect like a donkey. And if the Sun is in any of the houses of Saturn, especially in Aquarius, the child will be strong yet fearful, a lover of solitude and emptiness, enduring injuries and not seeking vengeance for them, for which reason he will be considered base by many. And if the Sun is in its descent, or moves less than its mean motion, or is diminished in number, the child will be weak in power, soft of heart, fearful, and of dull intellect like a donkey—especially if the Sun is in a watery sign. And with this—

# Venus has the governance of the fifth month from the casting of the seed into the womb. And it is the planet of beauty, shapeliness, and joy. Therefore, when in the fifth month Venus is in its ascension and in its own house—especially in Taurus—a child born from the first hour of the day until the third will have a beautiful face, complete limbs, a grand appearance, a ruddy complexion, and will be joyful, provoking men to laughter with his words. He will also be full of mercy, a seeker of beautiful things, and will adorn himself in the manner of women. And if, with this, Venus is in the face of Mars at the hour of the casting of the seed, a child born from the 4th hour until the 6th will have yellow hair and variegated eyes; he will be beautiful and sublime without fear, not restraining his anger. And if Venus is in the face of Saturn at the hour of the casting of the seed into the womb, a child born from the 7th hour until the ninth will be white, of a subtle color, beautiful in appearance, with curly hair; dry and thin of body, prone to much sickness, a traveler, and retaining injuries done to him in his breast; astute, fearing God little, a robber of others' goods, and ready to do evil. And if Venus is in the face of the Moon at the hour of the casting of the seed from the 10th hour until the 12th, the child will have a beautiful face but a deformed body; he will be of large stature, soft of flesh, and of much humidity and coldness; he will also be a fool of rude genius, a heavy donkey, fearful, and of little thought or knowledge. And likewise for the hours of the night, divide the day and judge by them. And if Venus, being in the face of Jupiter, is in the human signs—of which in this case Gemini and Libra are the best—the child will be tall and corpulent, of a beautiful body and face, having complete limbs, wise, chaste, and light; yet returning evil for evil, grave in his actions, and commendable in all things to be done. And if Venus, especially by night, is in the face of Mercury, the child will have a thin face, long jaws, and round eyes; he will be lean with little flesh, of a subtle body, a liar, given to much laughter and many words. And if Venus is in its descent and in the house and face of Saturn, especially in dark signs, the child will have an eclipsed body, a deformed face, a shameful appearance, heavy, sad, of little laughter, and always grieving. But if Venus is with any misfortune, or the Dragon's Tail in the face of Saturn, or is combust <note>So close to the Sun that its influence is "burned up" or weakened</note>, the child will be unfortunate in all his affairs, nor will anyone desire to come to him because of the diminishment of his limbs.

# Mercury has the governance of the sixth month from the casting of the seed into the womb. And it is the planet of doctrine, eloquence, and knowledge. When in the 6th month Mercury is in its ascension, the child will be of good eloquence, and his words will please men; he will be a secretary to great and powerful men. Easily, on slight occasion and without premeditation, he will provoke laughter; and this will be stronger if Mercury is in its own house or term. And if, with Mercury thus disposed, Jupiter looks upon it, he will be most eloquent, wise in books, and a wonderful orator, saying much upon a single word. And if Mercury is in the house or term of Jupiter, he will be of good eloquence, wise, and of subtle genius. And if Mercury is in the house or term of Venus, he will be wonderful in speech and tongue, mild in word, and one who will frequently ask questions. And if Mercury is in the house or term of Saturn, the child will be of a hindered tongue, so that he cannot explain his words well; yet he will be of sound thought and good intellect. But if Mercury, thus disposed, is in its descent, the child will be as if mute, especially if it is placed in signs lacking a voice.

# The Moon has the governance of the 7th month from the casting of the seed into the womb. And it is the planet that completes the limbs of the child and his perfection. When, therefore, one is born in that month, he will be complete in limbs because of the fulfillment of the planets' significations. And if the birth is delayed until the eighth month, the governance passes to Saturn. Those born in that month come out dead, or will die quickly. And if the birth is delayed until the ninth month, the governance returns to Jupiter; and he is the planet of life and movement; therefore the wind of Jupiter will move the child of God, the boy, and bring him forth into the world.

### On the manner of the newborn: what its nature will be: And whether it will be a human or a brute animal. Chapter 5.

**S**cias <note>Latin: "Know that..."</note> that the signification of the newborn's form is derived only from the movement of the planets and their change within human signs <note>Signs depicting humans, like Gemini or Virgo</note> and the parts and angles of the signs. When many planets are in the angles <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which are the strongest points in a chart</note>—especially the lord of the rising triplicity, the Sun, the Moon, the Part of Fortune, and the point of conjunction or opposition preceding the birth—it signifies a high manner for the newborn: its elements, nature, and human form. But if all these aforementioned, or the greater part of them, are falling <note>In "cadent" houses, which are weak</note> or unfortunate, and in the signs of beasts, it is a sign that the fetus will lack human form. ¶ Similarly, when the lord of the Ascendant and the Moon look upon the lord of the sign of life, and also look upon their own sign, it is a sign that the newborn will be monstrous, or will have the appearance of brute animals. And if, with this, the malefic planets look upon the lord of the sign of life, the nails and hair will be similar to cats and dogs. When these said malefics look upon the sign of life itself or its lord from an angle without the aspect of a fortune <note>A "fortune" refers to a benefic planet like Jupiter or Venus</note>, the newborn will be of the genus of beasts that hunt men. Hermes, who was the head of the wise, says: that when a masculine planet is in a feminine part of the Ascendant and in a feminine sign, and a malefic looks upon it from a feminine part and a feminine sign, and the birth is nocturnal, or Saturn and Mars look upon the said planet, or Mercury is unfortunate, or with Mars in a bad place and the Moon combust <note>Too close to the Sun, thus weakened</note> in the middle of a sign—that is, having no latitude and being in a sign of four-footed or eight-footed creatures—or if the Sun behaves in this manner, and also the lord of the human [sign] is unfortunate or falling or occidental or retrograde: it will be twisted in its rising; or if it is in the right part, that birth will not be of a man, but of some evil spirit. And do not say it is of the animals that devour men unless Jupiter looks upon Mars, or Mars provides testimony to Jupiter. But if Mars is with Saturn, the woman will give birth to a scorpion or a serpent, or some animal of a similar species. ¶ Furthermore, look at the part of the twelfth-part <note>The "duodenaria" or "dodecatemoria," a 2.5-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign</note> of the seven planets, and especially of the Sun and Moon. Look also at the Ascendant and what planet is in it and who looks upon it. And whether the Ascendant is of human figures or of beasts. When the twelfth-part of the planets falls in a sign of beasts, the newborn will be similar to beasts or monstrous. And this is stronger if the Sun or Moon are in the Ascendant, or the Part of Fortune, or the lord of its house. ¶ But if a fortune looks upon it, the newborn will not be similar to beasts, but a nourisher of beasts that devour men. ¶ Similarly, when the Moon does not look upon the lord of its house which is in its opposition, or the Moon is in a bestial sign and in aspect to a malefic, it is a sign that the newborn will be similar to one of the animals. ¶ And I will show you a certain similarity which I found in the books of the wise. There was a certain birth whose Ascendant was 18 degrees of Capricorn; and the Midheaven 11 degrees of Scorpio; and the opposition [preceding birth] at 9 degrees of Capricorn and 39 minutes; the Part of Fortune at 24 degrees of Taurus; the Sun at 29 degrees 54 minutes of Taurus; Jupiter at 24 degrees 14 minutes of Virgo; Mars at 4 degrees retrograde 16 minutes of Leo; and Mercury at 14 degrees retrograde 45 minutes of Taurus. ¶ Because Jupiter, the Moon, and Mercury are falling from the angles, and Saturn, the lord of the Ascendant, is in the opposite of his house, and Jupiter similarly; and because the Sun is in an angle yet falling; and Saturn, who is the lord of the house of the past opposition, is in the opposite of the said opposition and also the Ascendant: it is a sign that the woman would not give birth to a man but a brute animal. And because the Part of Fortune fell in a four-footed sign of the Moon's triplicity, which signifies food or nourishment, and is in a four-footed sign; and also the lord of the 3rd and the lord of the Moon's house, and the twelfth-part of the Moon are in a four-footed sign, and similarly the Ascendant is a four-footed sign—all this signifies that the brute animal would be a dog. And because the place of the Moon, which is the lady of the night, looked upon Jupiter and the lord of her house, and Venus, who is the lady of the Part of Fortune, looked upon the Part of Fortune... it is a sign that its nourishment would be in a good place. ¶ Similarly, there was a certain birth whose Ascendant was 16 degrees 32 minutes of Capricorn; the Midheaven 1 degree 34 minutes of Scorpio; the opposition preceding this birth was in the 20th degree and

<column-break/>

56 minutes of Sagittarius; Venus in 16 degrees 50 minutes of Taurus; Jupiter and the Moon in Taurus. Look at the lord of the opposition preceding the said birth. Because the said part is separated from him: look at the lord of the Moon's triplicity, who is the lord of the said part, how they are diverse and one does not look upon the other; and how they are separated from the Ascendant and from the lord of its house. ¶ Similarly, look at Saturn, who is the lord of the Part of Fortune and the Ascendant, how he falls from the angles. Then look at the twelfth-part of the Sun and Moon. And how it is placed in a four-footed sign and how all the planets are falling from the angles and separated, except the Moon and Jupiter, which are in the house of the end of the matter under the earth <note>The 4th House</note>: all this signified that the woman would give birth to a donkey. And because the Moon is in the triplicity of the Ascendant with Jupiter, and in her exaltation, it is a sign that the donkey would be of beautiful nourishment and manner.

->**On the Face of the Newborn. Chapter 6.**<-

**S**cias <note>Know that...</note> that the ancient wise men [wrote] much regarding the house of the face of men and their colors according to what they inherit from the disposition of the firmament and its signification. ¶ But some of them said that it is nothing. ¶ Where they indeed affirmed and verified it. ¶ Those who feel the contrary argue thus: ¶ Do you not see that someone is born in the land of the blacks, and you already know the manner of his parents and the blackness of the city in which he was born; and yet you found the Ascendant and its lord, as well as the lord of the face of the Ascendant and other planets contrary in the signification of his color—that he ought to be white or red, or having white eyes in the manner of the Italians or Gauls? ¶ It stands that they have spoken in this way of believing, and it pertains to worthy men; yet let them not fall into error, for if the birth of an "Auri" (that is, of some Ethiopian) signified whiteness, we ought not for this reason judge him to be white or red, but that he will be less black than his parents. ¶ And similarly for some Italian or Gaul, if a strong Saturn signifies blackness, we ought not to judge that he will be black like a Moor, but he will not be as white as his parents; for those who do the contrary fall into error before those testing them, who will make them appear foolish; they defame them and take the opportunity to condemn their science. ¶ For we find in the books of the wise that the births of sons are entwined with the births of the fathers, and colors are changed according to the site of the lands; for whiteness and blackness will be according to the disposition of the Sun in its approach to or distance from regions. Except that some planets add or diminish in the colors of the newborns. ¶ Thus we find some black and some blacker. Also some white and some whiter, and so with other colors according to the diversity of the Ascendant and the signification of the planets relative to the color of the citizens of some city or region or the root of the parentage. Andozoar <note>Likely referring to the Andalusian physician Avenzoar (Ibn Zuhr)</note> said that the diversity of human color is difficult to explain, and we do not find anyone who has explained them. ¶ Ptolemy said in the Great Book of Planets <note>The Almagest or Tetrabiblos</note> that in every hour 24 thousand "roboaz" <note>A small unit of time or degree</note> ascend, and every "roboat" pertains to 10 thousand moments, and every moment has its own color, taste, and nature. ¶ And this is so subtle that human sense could not scrutinize it; no man knows this except God alone. When you wish to know the color of the newborn in this way, look at the Ascendant: and if you find there its lord, or the lord of its exaltation, triplicity, or term: judge by him. When there are many planets in the Ascendant: look at the one that is of the face <note>The "face" or "decan," a 10-degree subdivision of a sign</note> of the Ascendant; and judge according to its signification, mixing it however with the signification of the face of the lord of the Ascendant and the planet to which the lord of the face sends its strength. ¶ And if there is no planet in the Ascendant: take first the lord of the face of the Ascendant if he looks upon the Ascendant; but if he is falling from the Ascendant: look at the planet closer to the Ascendant, or the one that looks upon the Ascendant with a better aspect, such as from its house, exaltation, triplicity, or term; and mix the colors of the planets with the colors of the signs and the terms in which the planets were. And declare the color or form of the face according to the color of the planet, and the form or color of the body according to the color of the sign. ¶ Know therefore that beauty is attributed to Jupiter and Venus. ¶ To the Sun and Moon, however, subtlety and clarity. ¶ To Mars and Saturn, however, ugliness. ¶ Mercury, being with a beautiful planet, offers beauty; with an ugly one, ugliness. Similarly, the houses of the fortunes are called beautiful, and those of the misfortunes, ugly. ¶ Also days and bright stars help in beauty and subtlety; nights [do the opposite].

## On the manners and colors of the planets. Chapter 7.

<term>♄</term> **Saturn** by his nature makes the native <note>The "native" refers to the person born under this astrological influence.</note> pale or dark and of a coarse color, with thick eyes. One of the eyes will be somewhat squinting or larger than the other; he will have much hair and a large face, specifically large jaws; and his gaze will be cast down toward the earth. Along with this, the native will be sad in mind, foul in face, having a bad look, disordered teeth, and shaggy hair.

<term>♃</term> **Jupiter** by his nature makes one tall, declining somewhat toward blackness; the eyes large and prominent. The pupils will be small, and the hair will not be perfectly curly, nor perfectly straight, but middling. His beard will be curly. His manner will be handsome. He will hold his head and jaws somewhat elevated; the nose large and even. In flesh and stature, he will be well-proportioned, and his face cheerful.

<term>♂</term> **Mars** by his nature makes the native ruddy, with red or inflamed eyes and a round face. And perhaps he will have a red mark on the face or a scar; or he will have a large nose. He will also be sharp and of subtle wit, full of anger, and handsome in his uprightness. His face is somewhat swollen. He is envious, sad, and prone to cursing and evil thoughts; his fingers are long and with little flesh; his chest is sharp; in his gait he is long-striding, and in his heart, he is marvelous.

<term>☉</term> **Sun** by his nature makes the native large. The whiteness of the eyes will be small, the face round, and the head large. His color is shining. The forehead is ample. The eyes are prominent. He has a loud voice and is of equal stature to conquerors; he speaks of others without shame in a mocking way. His gaze carries lordship. In youth, he will have yellow hair, but afterward, he will become bald.

<term>♀</term> **Venus** by his nature makes the native of a shining color mixed with some redness; with beautiful eyes whose gaze will be decorous and sweet, like the glittering dawn. The eyebrows are small, the head round. The blackness of the eyes will be greater than the whiteness. The nose <note>original: "Dos", likely a typo for "Nasus"</note> is subtle and small; the cheeks fleshy; the neck handsome; the chest sharp; the fingers short; the legs large; and generally, the whiteness of the native's eyes tends toward blackness.

<term>♿</term> **Mercury** by his nature makes the native of a white color tending toward greenness; his face will be narrow; the jaws comely; the eyebrows joined; the nostrils beautiful; the mouth wide. His eyes are mobile, the teeth small, the beard sparse. The face is beautiful. He will be very lean. His stature is even, his steps short, his limbs subtle. He possesses much sense and intellect, and utters many good words.

<term>☾</term> **Moon** by her nature makes the native of a white color, whose face will be round. The look is decorous; the beard mobile; yet of a beautiful and clear color. The body is healthy and complete in its limbs. Know therefore these stated manners and colors of the planets, and mix them with the manners and colors of the signs.

## On the signs and the colors of the signs.

<term>♈︎</term> **Aries** by its nature makes the native of a color tending toward whiteness; his hair will be curly; his body <note>original: "Titius", likely "Titus" or "Totus"</note> not straight; the face long; the eyes large; the ears small; and the neck large.

<term>♉︎</term> **Taurus** by its nature makes the native of a reddish color with an ample forehead, a long nose with wide nostrils, and large eyes; his hair will be curly, upright, and black. His limbs are diverse. The upper part of the neck is thick and strong; his gait is beautiful. But his sense will be weak.

<term>♊︎</term> **Gemini** by its nature makes the native of a mixed color and equal stature, with an ample chest. He will be faithful, and his mastery will revolve around writing or computation.

<term>♋︎</term> **Cancer** by its nature makes the native of a pale color, with thick limbs and a hairy neck; the lower parts of his body will be larger than the upper parts. He will have a large forehead, distorted teeth, curly hair, and a beard. He will be short in stature with small eyes and broad shoulders.

<term>♌︎</term> **Leo** by its nature makes the native of a yellow color with yellow hair and a beautiful stature, whose upper parts will be larger than the lower; the chest ample; the vision sharp; the face grave; the legs subtle. He will be famous but of bad encounter.

<term>♍︎</term> **Virgo** by its nature makes the native of a black color, of beautiful and equal stature, and handsome in limbs.

<term>♎︎</term> **Libra** by its nature makes the native two-colored, of handsome stature; in flesh, height, and manner, he will be balanced.

<term>♏︎</term> **Scorpio** by its nature makes the native of an ashen-red color, with white and small eyes tending toward blackness; much hair; a long circumference of a small face. The feet are large, the body light. He is one who will sow discord among others and will be evil.

<term>♐︎</term> **Sagittarius** by its nature makes the native of a white-yellow color, with white or yellow hair, a subtle face, and a long beard. He has a large belly, and his posterior part will be more handsome to look upon than the anterior.

<term>♑︎</term> **Capricorn** by its nature makes the native of a black color, hairy, with a dry body, a long face, a sharp beard, and subtle ears.

<term>♒︎</term> **Aquarius** by its nature makes the native of a greenish-black color, short stature, great spirit, and great goodness; liberal with men. He will be of great expense and a prodigal. He will have one leg larger than the other; his blood and color will be apparent.

<term>♓︎</term> **Pisces** by its nature makes the native of a white color, with a small head and a comely beard. The jaws are ample, the chest large, and his limbs will be poorly disposed; he will become either lame or hunchbacked. And know that the signs ascending directly make natives of large bodies by their nature; those ascending crookedly make them small.

---

### To whom the age of the child will be likened: namely, to the father or mother, paternal uncle or maternal uncle. Chapter 8.

When you wish to know whom the native will resemble: look at the face of the ascendant <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth.</note> and which planet is its lord. For if the Sun is the lord of said face, the native will be like the father; and if the Moon is its mistress, the native will be like the mother. Specifically, if the lord of the face of the Ascendant is the lord of the Fourth House, and the native is a son, he will be like the father. If it is a daughter, she will be like the mother. If the lord of the face of the ascendant is the lord of the place of the Sun, he will be like the paternal uncle. If it is the lord of the place of the Moon, he will be like the maternal uncle. Work in the remaining matters according to this mode. And having said these things, let us descend to the nutrition of children.

---

### On the nutrition of children. Chapter 9.

Know that there are four ways of being born. <insert>First</insert> is that one is born dead. <insert>Second</insert>, that one is born alive but will not live nor be nourished. <insert>Third</insert>, that one is born alive and is nourished and lives, but does not reach old age. <insert>Fourth</insert>, that one is born alive and lives and is nourished and reaches a good old age. To know these four modes, it is necessary that you know the divisions that follow, which are these: <insert>The Ascendant</insert> and its lord; <insert>The Part of Fortune</insert> and its lord; <insert>The Sun and Moon; conjunction</insert> and <insert>Opposition</insert>. The Ascendant signifies the likeness of the mode of the native, his appearance, and his descent after his hiding. <insert>The Part of Fortune</insert> is called the ascendant of the Moon, just as the ascendant of the nativity is called the ascendant of the Sun.

The Sun is the light of heaven and the candle; the King of the stars and their disposer; the helper of the seasons and the direction of life, of beasts, trees, herbs, and bodies.

The Moon, by the circuit of her orb and her proximity to the world, takes precedence in the likeness of men, in their beginning and completion, and also in their diminution, destruction, and annulment; she is a partner in the significance of the stars and planets, and she has increase and decrease according to the likeness of the native in his birth. Conjunction is called the likeness of the parents who hold themselves toward the native in the manner of joining and separation and the light that proceeds from them. Prevention or opposition of the Moon is called the likeness of the native when he is in his strength and in the middle of his life; which, while it passes, begins to diminish daily until it reaches the end of his life. And these things cannot be known except by the mode of triplicity, namely in its strength and weakness; also <term>Aliebel</term> and <term>Alicher</term> <note>Likely corrupted Arabic astrological terms referring to planetary dignity or "Hyleg/Alcocoden" (givers of life).</note> in their firmness and fall, because the lords of triplicity signify the ordering of day and night, and the change of the native occurs with the succession of night to day and day to night. Therefore, a native who is born dead will be one who has the three significators of the lords of triplicity falling from the angles in his nativity; especially if any of the misfortunes <note>Malefic planets like Mars or Saturn.</note> has lordship in the ascendant for evil. For then it will signify the destruction and fall of the native. When the Moon is in the west without the aspect of any fortune <note>Benefic planets like Jupiter or Venus.</note>, and a fortune is in one of the angles looking upon her, the one who is born will not live one hour. Namely, when the Moon is in the fourth house with one of the fortunes and some fortune in the seventh house...
