# On the Vitality and Upbringing of the Newborn

If the Moon looks upon <term>Saturn</term><note>original: "Saturno"; the planet associated with restriction, cold, and death</note>, the child will come forth dead. When the sign in which the conjunction or prevention preceding the birth occurred, the <term>Alcocoden</term><note>The "giver of life," a planet used to determine the length of a person's life</note>, the lord of the Sun's term, the Moon, the Ascendant, the lord of the Sun's <term>triplicity</term><note>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, Water)</note> by day and the Moon's by night, and the <term>Part of Fortune</term><note>A mathematical point in the chart representing well-being and luck</note> are all unfortunate, the child will be born dead. 

¶ Look to the lord of the luminaries <note>The Sun and Moon</note> by day and night, whether any planet is joined to it; also look to the place of the Part of Fortune and the Part of Cutting <note>The "Pars Abscissionis," associated with the end of life</note> to see if they are in fixed or falling houses, and who their lords are. For if they are under the Sun's rays and are beheld by the "unfortunates" <note>Mars and Saturn</note>, or joined to them, or joined among themselves, the child shall not taste life. This significance will be worse if the unfortunates behold the Sun and Moon or are bound with them. For if it be so, the child shall not live for a single moment. When all the planets or the greater part of them are in the 6th, 8th, or 12th house <note>The "evil" houses associated with illness, death, and hidden enemies</note>, and the lord of the Ascendant does not behold them, the child shall not taste life. 

When the Moon is not received by the unfortunates, and an unfortunate planet is in the angle of the Ascendant or the tenth house, while the "fortunes" <note>Jupiter and Venus</note> are falling, the child shall not taste life. Especially if the Moon is in any angle and with an unfortunate, or if the lord of the Ascendant or the degree of the Ascendant is in the 4th house or in opposite aspect to an unfortunate. If an unfortunate is free from all impediment and beholds the degree of the Ascendant or the Moon from any angle: in this case, the child shall have life, by the **Will of God**. 

When the Moon is in the middle of the sky <note>The Midheaven or 10th house</note> in aspect to a fortune with <term>Mars</term>, it signifies that the child will come forth dead and in vain. When the Moon and Mars are in the Ascendant and Saturn is in the West <note>The 7th house</note>, the child will come forth dead, especially if a fortune does not behold the Moon. And if the Moon is in the West with Mars and Saturn is in the Ascendant, the child shall not live a whole day.

¶ The child who is born and sees the world, but will not be nourished <note>i.e., will not survive infancy</note>, occurs when the significators and lords of the triplicity are in angles, but the greater part of the others are falling from the angles and unfortunate. 

¶ **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of astrology</note> said that when the luminaries do not behold the Ascendant, or the Moon is under the rays of the Sun, the child shall live but a little while. 

¶ The Moon joined to Saturn in his triplicity, or in the 4th house or in opposite aspect to him, signifies that the child shall live but will be of poor nourishment <note>sickly or poorly raised</note>. And if the Moon is with Saturn and the Sun, the child shall not be nourished. And if the Moon is with the Sun and Mars, or besieged between Mars and the Sun, the child shall be of short life. When the Moon is in her descent and in the West, the child shall not live nor even be nourished, especially if the Moon, being outside the angles, is made unfortunate by Saturn. 

When the Moon is between two fortunes and a fortune does not behold her, the child shall live but a little while. When Mars and Saturn are in the Ascendant and the fortunes are falling from the Ascendant or from the sign of conjunction or prevention, and the unfortunates behold those two places, the child shall not be nourished; especially if the fortunes do not behold the Moon and Sun. When the Moon is in the same degree as the Sun, and Saturn beholds them from the 4th house or in opposite aspect, the child shall not be nourished. When the Moon is in Aries and in opposition to Mars, and a fortune does not behold her, the child shall be of weak nourishment and short life. 

When the Ascendant and the lord of the place of the Sun and Moon and the Part of Fortune are under the rays of the Sun, the child shall be of little life. When the Moon is in Capricorn and both unfortunates are in the 4th house from the Ascendant, the child shall not be nourished. When the Moon, diminished in light, is besieged between two unfortunates, the child shall live but a little while. When the unfortunates behold each other in opposite aspect, so that one is in the Ascendant and the other is in the West, and the Moon beholds them from the middle of the sky or the 4th house, the child shall not be nourished. When any of the unfortunates are in the 7th house, and the Sun and Moon, being "pilgrims" <note>Planets in a sign where they have no essential dignity</note>, behold them, the child shall not be nourished. 

When the Moon is in the term and aspect of an unfortunate in an angle, and a fortune does not behold her, the child shall not be nourished. When Saturn is in the Ascendant and Mars is in the 7th house, the child shall live but a little while, and for as long as he lives, he shall have evil. When Saturn is likewise in the Ascendant and the Moon, being a pilgrim, is in the West, the child shall live but a little while and shall be deformed. When <term>Venus</term> is in her fall in the West and Saturn is in the West, the child shall not be nourished. 

And some have said that if any of the unfortunates cast their rays upon the degree of the Ascendant, the child shall not escape that hour, unless the fortunes help by casting their rays upon the same prominent place, even if the Moon and the lord of the Ascendant and the triplicity are free from the unfortunates. When the Moon is with an unfortunate in the 7th or 4th house, the nourishment shall be bad, especially if the said unfortunate casts its rays upon the degree of the Ascendant and the lord of the triplicity is falling in the 7th house. 

When the Moon is impeded by the unfortunates and in her fall, and you wish to know if the child shall be nourished: Consider if the circuit of the Moon is made through the places of the unfortunates and returned to the Ascendant and applying to the unfortunates, and afterwards passes to the fortunes. If the child is not dead then, he shall live until 12 revolutions <note>years</note> are completed. 

The infant who shall be nourished and live, but shall not reach old age, is one who has eight significations and the lords of the triplicity unequal—that is, some shall be fortunate and some unfortunate, or some strong and others falling. And specifically the two lords of the triplicity of the Ascendant, namely the first and the second. When any of them is in a good place and has many testimonies—such as if the Sun is the lord of the triplicity of the Ascendant and the lord of the Part of Fortune, and free from the unfortunates and from fall—you shall judge life and nourishment for the child, but he shall not reach old age. 

¶ **Bozochius** <note>An Arabic astrologer, likely Al-Buzjani or similar</note> said that if the divider of life is in a good place, it signifies the child's stay in the world and good nourishment. When <term>Jupiter</term> is in the Ascendant or beholds it with a trine aspect, the child shall be well nourished. And likewise if Jupiter is in any of the angles or succeeding houses, and Venus, free from the unfortunates, retrogradation, and combustion <note>being too close to the Sun</note>, does not behold him, the child shall be nourished and live, but shall not reach old age. And if in a daytime birth Saturn is in the angles or the triplicity of the Ascendant, the child shall live and be nourished. 

When the Moon and <term>Mercury</term> are in the 7th house and Jupiter is in the 4th, the child shall live. When the Moon is free from the unfortunates, descent, and peregrination, and the lords of the triplicity of the Ascendant are in their own houses or exaltations, or in bright degrees, not retrograde or combust, nor even made unfortunate, the child shall be nourished and live, especially when he prevails in his exaltation. And when the Moon is in her own [sign] and increasing in motion with a fortune and free from Mars—for Mars destroys nourishment, especially in daytime births and specifically when he is above the earth. 

When Saturn and Mars are in the Ascendant or another strong and fortunate place, yet appearing from under the rays of the Sun, they signify healthy nourishment and the child's stay in the world. And the same is signified by the daytime [planets] in daytime signs and nighttime [planets] in nighttime signs, being in the angle of the Ascendant or in the middle of the sky. When [a planet] has crossed the middle of the sign in which it is, and that sign is the Ascendant or the tenth, the child shall live and be nourished but shall not reach old age. When the lord of the triplicity of the Ascendant is strong and in the middle of the sky or in some strength or in the 4th house, it signifies the beauty of nourishment by the **Will of God**. 

The infant who shall live and be nourished and reach old age is the one who has the aforementioned significators fixed and strong in angles and free, not falling from the angles. And likewise when the Alcocoden of the birth is strong and stable. And it is clear that if it is struck in places where it has some dignity, it is strong and safe from retrogradation and misfortune. For then the Alcocoden shall give him his "greater years" <note>The maximum lifespan assigned to a planet in astrology</note>. 

When the Sun and Moon are fortunate in the succeeding houses, the child shall enter into years and shall have well-being, and shall master his own nature. When the Moon is in the middle of the sky and in aspect of a fortune, and the unfortunates are falling from her, the child shall have a long stay in the world. When the fortunes are in the 4th house, the child shall reach old age and his death shall be in a great state. When Jupiter is in his own house and his own term, it signifies length of life, so that the child shall see his children's children and their children. 

When the Sun is in the term of Saturn and in his own house or exaltation, the child shall make a long stay in the world and shall have greatness like a mountain. When the Sun is in his own house or dignity so that the unfortunates do not behold him, the child shall live long and be the master of his nature. When the Sun, free from unfortunates, is in the middle of the sky, the child shall have a long life with high status. 

When Venus is in the Ascendant and the unfortunates are falling from her, and Jupiter beholds her, the child shall be of long stay and a beautiful life in this world. When the Moon is in the Ascendant without the aspect of an unfortunate, the child shall be of long life. When Mercury, Venus, and the Sun behold the Moon with a good aspect, the child shall be of long life and equal and healthy in his nature. When the Moon and Mercury are in the same sign, and also in the West or the 3rd house, the child shall be of long life. When the Part of Fortune or its lord is oriental and outside the rays, without the aspect of an unfortunate in the Ascendant, the child shall be of long life. 

Certain wise men, in the matter of nourishment, look to the three states of the Moon: namely on the 3rd, 7th, and 40th days from birth. For the Moon in these periods transmits the nature of all things and signs...

And so, when three days are completed, the newborn will begin to suck milk and recognize its mother, and will begin to perceive taste in the sweetness of the milk. After three years, he will begin to walk on his own feet and speak; and his intellect will increase. And in another 7 days, the Moon will complete another part of the circle and change its complexion. The signs will signify by their nature the abundance or scarcity of milk, and the weakness or strength of the nursing. ¶ When the newborn has passed 7 days and there is confidence of good [planetary] support, he will have good nutrition and a long stay in this world, and will be free from evil diseases. Then, if he is the son of a Jew or a Moor <note>original: "mauri"; likely referring to Muslims/Saracens</note>, he shall be circumcised. ¶ And if he is the son of a Christian, he shall be baptized or catechized. ¶ And when the newborn reaches 7 years, then he shall discern and receive the teaching of the heavens and his own life and matters of faith. ¶ In 40 years, he will complete the years of strength and nature, after which he will begin to decline daily until he completes the life which God gave. ¶ Know that when the Moon is 3 days from the falling angles <note>cadent houses</note> and is joined to a fortune by conjunction or aspect, it signifies a scarcity of milk and difficulty in nutrition. ¶ When the significator of nutrition is an infortune, specifically Saturn, and he is peregrine <note>a planet in a position where it has no essential dignity/strength</note>, it signifies the destruction of the newborn and that he will live in a disordered way. ¶ The Sun in a daytime birth existing in the term of Saturn signifies that there will be labor and foulness in the nutrition of the newborn. ¶ When Saturn and Mars cast their rays upon the term of the Ascendant, they signify misfortune and [poor] nutrition. ¶ When the Part of Fortune is misfortuned in the 4th house, it signifies bad nutrition. ¶ When the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of Nutrition are bound with the Part of Fortune, and none of them is strong in its place, they signify the damnation of the nourishment. ¶ When the Part of Fortune is in the 11th house and its Lord is without reception, the newborn will be difficult to nourish and will add evil upon evil. ¶ When the Moon is separated from an infortune, it signifies many diseases for the newborn during the time of nursing, and he will have labor in his nourishment, and the newborn will have wounds in his body and pains in the blood.

### Regarding the significance of the faces of the signs concerning nutrition.

¶ The first face of Aries signifies diseases in nutrition and the pain of the stone <note>kidney or bladder stones</note>, and that rot shall exit from his flesh. ¶ The second face of Aries will multiply disease, but he will be protected from them and healed. ¶ He who is born in the 3rd face of Aries will fall ill after 5 months from birth. ¶ He who is born in the first face of Taurus, pains and diseases will invade him for a year and a half, persisting until he reaches the end of his life. And when he is 30 months old, he will have hot pains. And when he is six years old, he will have disordered diseases and things regarding the blood. ¶ He who is born in the 2nd face of Taurus will be healthy until 7 years from birth have passed, and then diseases will invade him, but by the will of God, he will be freed from them. ¶ He who is born in the first face of Gemini will fall ill with a great disease for 5 months from birth. And when he is one year and a half, he will fall from one disease into another until 3 and a half years, and then the itch <note>scabies/skin rash</note> will invade him. ¶ For him born in the 2nd face of Gemini, from the second month of his age, diseases will not be removed from him until he reaches a year and a half. And when he is four years old, he will fall ill with such a disease that his death will be doubted. ¶ He who is born in the 3rd face of Leo will be sick after six months from birth, and wounds will appear in his flesh and the pain of the stone; nor will it be removed from him until he reaches the 7th year of his age. ¶ He who is born in the 2nd face of Leo, and also the 3rd, will be ill for 7 years from the hour of birth, counting daily. ¶ He who is born in the first face of Libra will be nourished by the milk of two women. And when he is three months of age, pains will find him. When nine months have passed, he will have a flow of blood from the nostrils. ¶ When he reaches the first face within a year and a half, he will fall ill with a disease; from which when he is healed, pains in the head will invade him for two years, 6 months, and 20 days. ¶ He who is born in the first face of Scorpio, after 5 years from birth, pains will invade him. He who is born in the 1st face of Sagittarius, after 25 months from birth, will have great diseases so that his death will be doubted; and for 10 months he will be sick in the head, but will be freed by a flow of the belly <note>diarrhea/purging</note>. ¶ When he enters the tenth year, he will fall ill with a heavy disease, from which if he prevails, he will live long. ¶ He who is born in the 1st face of Sagittarius <note>Note: the text repeats the sign, likely a scribal error for a different face or sign</note>, when he reaches 4 months of his birth, diseases will invade him. And when he reaches the second year, he will be sick with a disease; from which if he prevails, he will be of long life. ¶ He who is born in the 3rd face of Sagittarius, pains and diseases will invade him until 4 months of his age have passed. And if he prevails, he will live much. ¶ He who is born in the first face of Capricorn, his death will be doubted until two years from his birth have passed. ¶ He who is born in the 3rd face of Capricorn will be sick with a disease and will have many flatulences, and his parents will die; a strange woman will nourish him. ¶ He who is born in the first face of Aquarius will fall ill after 10 months from birth and will live after this, and will have sufficient nutrition. ¶ He who is born in the 2nd face of Aquarius will fall ill with a disease after 6 months from birth. ¶ He who is born in the 3rd face of Aquarius, disease will not be removed from him until 7 years from his birth have passed; afterward he will be healed and his life will be prolonged.

### Regarding the protection of the newborn. Chapter 10.

¶ Look at the Moon: if she is with a fortune in any of the angles or succedent houses, and an infortune is [absent], the newborn will be nourished. But his mother will look after him. ¶ If this Moon is in the term of an infortune, both parents will provide for him. ¶ If Mars in a daytime birth is in the 4th sign after the Sun, and the Moon is peregrine and beholds him from the 7th house, the newborn will be protected. ¶ And in the same way, you shall judge by Saturn in a nighttime birth.

### Regarding the beauty of the newborn's nourishment. Chapter 11.

¶ It should be held that separation from an infortune signifies the beauty of nourishment and that the newborn will be fortunate among brothers and the works of his brothers will be directed. ¶ If a fortune is with the Moon so that Jupiter beholds her in a daytime birth and Venus in a nighttime birth, it signifies beauty of nourishment; especially if the said planet is oriental and in a masculine sign in a daytime birth: his strength will be firm and healthy in the matter of nourishment. And say the same for a planet being occidental and in a feminine sign in a nighttime birth. ¶ And just as we judge the beauty of nourishment by the Part of Fortune, so we can judge by the Part of Absence. ¶ The Part of Absence is taken in the day from the Moon to the Sun, and in the night the opposite, and is projected from the Ascendant; and wherever the term falls, there the said part shall be. If it is with the Moon or any of the fortunes, or beholds the Moon, the newborn will be of beautiful stature and nutrition, and his teeth will grow without pain.

### Regarding the knowledge of the Hyleg and the Alcochoden. Chapter 12.

¶ The <term>Hyleg</term> <note>The "giver of life," a specific point in a birth chart used to determine longevity.</note> signifies the life of the newborn and the <term>Alcochoden</term> <note>The "giver of years," the planet that rules the Hyleg and determines the number of years a person might live.</note> signifies the years. And by its <term>Athazir</term> <note>A method of progression or moving points in the chart over time.</note> toward the aspect of fortunes or infortunes, you shall know the life and death of the newborn by the will of God. ¶ And know that the wise men extract the Hyleg from 5 places. ¶ First from the Sun, 2nd from the Moon, 3rd from the sign of the Ascendant, 4th from the Part of Fortune, and 5th from the place of the conjunction or prevention <note>the New or Full Moon</note> preceding the birth. ¶ When the birth is by day, let us begin first from the Sun, because it rules the present times and is the light of the day. ¶ If the birth is by night, let us begin first from the Moon, which is the light of the night and possesses the ordering of the night. ¶ If the Sun in a daytime birth is in the Ascendant, or the 10th, or the 11th house (which is the house of confidence and fortune), whether the sign be masculine or feminine, it is fit to be the Hyleg, provided the Lord of the House, Exaltation, Triplicity, or Term beholds it. ¶ But if none of these Lords behold it, the Sun cannot be the Hyleg. ¶ And if the Sun is not in these places, but in the 7th, 8th, or 9th house in a masculine sign, it will be fit to be the Hyleg, provided any of the aforesaid Lords behold it. ¶ Ptolemy, however, does not want the Sun existing in the 8th house to be the Hyleg; but if it is in the 4th house and in a masculine sign, it is fit to be the Hyleg, provided any of the aforesaid Lords behold it. ¶ In the same way, Ptolemy denies that the Moon in the 8th house can be the Hyleg, but it may be in the holy places <note>the other houses mentioned</note> provided it is in a feminine sign and any of the aforesaid Lords behold it. ¶ When the Sun is the Hyleg, the planet beholding it will be the Alcochoden. ¶ In nighttime births, we begin first from the Moon, as was said: if she is in the 3rd, 4th, 5th, or 8th house and in a feminine sign, she will be fit to be the Hyleg, provided any of the aforesaid Lords behold her. And the one who beholds her is called the Alcochoden. ¶ Afterward, seek the Athazir of the Hyleg and its aspect to the fortunes or infortunes and their rays. ¶ But if the Sun and Moon are not fit to be the Hyleg, see whether the degree of the Ascendant is free from infortunes, and its Lord is strong and free, and existing in the angles or succedents, not retrograde nor falling nor combust; then it will be fit to be the Hyleg, and from this take the Athazir. ¶ But if the degree of the Ascendant cannot be the Hyleg, look at the Part of Fortune: if it is in the angles or succedents and free from infortunes, and any of the aforesaid Lords behold it, it will be fit to be the Hyleg, and the one beholding it the Alcochoden; then from that take the Athazir and the times through it. ¶ But if the Part of Fortune is not fit to be the Hyleg, look at the conjunction or prevention which preceded the birth of the newborn. And if it is free from the impediments we have mentioned, and any of its Lords behold it, it will be fit to be the Hyleg. ¶ Look therefore at the degree in...

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological longevity, specifically focusing on the calculation of the Hyleg (the giver of life) and the Alcocoden (the giver of years), following the rules of classical and medieval authorities like Dorotheus and Ptolemy.</meta>

<page-num>6</page-num>
<sig>B i</sig>

From wherever the conjunction or prevention <note>The "prevention" refers to the Full Moon preceding birth; the "conjunction" refers to the New Moon.</note> occurred, take the **Athazir** <term>Athazir: a method of progression or "moving" a point in the chart over time to predict events</term> from that point, just as we said regarding the degrees of the Ascendant: giving one year to each degree. And if the **Athazir** reaches a "malefic" <note>original: "infortunā"; refers to Mars or Saturn</note> and a "benefic" <note>original: "fortuna"; refers to Jupiter or Venus</note> does not cast an aspect there: the native shall die in that year. Certain wise men have said that if in a daytime birth the Sun cannot be the **Hyleg** <term>Hyleg: the "Giver of Life," a specific planet or point used to determine the lifespan</term>, we ought to look to the Moon: which, if it be in the Ascendant or in the "angles" <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> or in a place where it has some dignity, and is in a feminine sign, it will be fit to be the **Hyleg**. And similarly in a nocturnal birth: when the Moon cannot be the **Hyleg**, look to the Sun when it is in the "angle of the earth" <note>The 4th house, the lowest point of the chart</note>. If it is in the 5th house, it will be a fit **Hyleg**, and then look for the **Alcocoden** <term>Alcocoden: the planet that determines the number of years the Hyleg grants</term>. Dorotheus said: When none of the other five aforementioned points can be the **Hyleg**, and the **Part of Fortune** is in the Ascendant and its lord is fortunate within the Ascendant after the 25th degree of the angle, it shall be fit to be the **Hyleg**. Also, if the Sun and Moon are in the 9th house and are not afflicted by malefics, they shall be fit to be **Hyleg**, provided that in a daytime birth the Sun is in a masculine sign and the Moon in a feminine one, and they are in their own houses or places where they have some dignity: as if Leo were ascending and the Sun in Aries, or Cancer ascending and the Moon in Pisces. For when they are in their own house, exaltation, triplicity, or term, they possess strength. Regarding the **Hyleg**, however, whether it is taken from the degree of the Ascendant, the **Part of Fortune**, or the preceding conjunction or opposition of the birth, we ought not to look at whether the sign is masculine or feminine, or whether the birth was by day or night. But rather, whether its lords aspect the **Hyleg** itself. Therefore, when you have the **Hyleg**, calculate its **Athazir** to the locations of the malefics; for whenever it reaches a square <note>original: "4."</note> or opposite aspect of a malefic, and a benefic does not project its rays there, the death of the native is to be feared. But if a benefic projects its rays to that place (that is, to the malefic), the native will suffer some great impediment or sickness, but at last shall be delivered by the will of God. Others, however, have said that if in a daytime birth the Sun cannot be **Hyleg**, nor the Moon in a nocturnal birth, and the birth was "conjunctional" <note>Born following a New Moon</note>, then we ought to take the degree of the Ascendant. And if it was "preventional" <note>Born following a Full Moon</note>, take the **Part of Fortune** and work with the **Athazir** as Ptolemy said: because although the Moon may not be the **Hyleg**, it is still necessary that its **Athazir** be calculated toward the benefics or malefics. For when it reaches a malefic, it will do one of two things: either induce an evil similar to death, or it will kill the effect of the benefics; if it signifies death, it will assist it. For when the Moon is **Hyleg** and the degree of the Ascendant in the root of the nativity is afflicted, and it comes together with the Moon or Sun or a conjunction of a malefic at the time of birth, the native will be in danger of death. If the degree of the Ascendant is **Hyleg** and is afflicted in the root of the nativity, or joins itself with the lord of the Ascendant or of the conjunction when afflicted: the native shall have a great sickness, so that his death shall be doubted. If you calculate the **Athazir** by the degrees of the conjunction or prevention toward the aspects of the benefics and malefics, and the **Athazir** joins itself with one of the malefics, the death of the native is to be feared. And when the **Athazir** joins itself to the "term" <note>A specific subdivision of a zodiac sign</note> of a malefic, it will kill someone, or induce an evil similar to killing; unless a strong benefic aspects that same term, or the Sun, being strong in the nativity, reaches that point with which the malefic is joined. For that shall signify deliverance by the will of God. When you find the **Hyleg**, and one of the lords having dignity in the **Hyleg** aspects it, make that planet the **Alcocoden**; and according to the strength of its position, it will give years, and also its power to which it has given its strength through conjunction or aspect. But if none of the aforesaid lords aspects the **Hyleg**, the native shall have no **Alcocoden**; you should then calculate the **Alcafir** <note>Another method of time-distribution</note> with the degree of the Ascendant from the place of the malefics. And when it reaches a malefic signifying the death of the native, that shall be the term of the native's death. And some have treated the **Hyleg** as they did the **Alcocoden**, by adding or subtracting years according to those aspecting the **Hyleg**. And know that if the lord of the house of the **Hyleg** aspects it, he is to be preferred over the lord of the exaltation; and the lord of the triplicity over the lord of the term; and the lord of the term shall act as the **Alcocoden**. Thus, whichever planet has more dignities in the place of the **Hyleg** is more fit to be the **Alcocoden** than another who has fewer dignities. Know therefore that if the **Alcocoden** is in the 10th or 1st house, strong and free from malefics, and also in its own house, exaltation, triplicity, or term: it will give the native its "great years." And if it is a "pilgrim" <note>A planet with no essential dignity in its current position</note> in said places, it will give its "middle years." And if it is retrograde, or combust <note>Too close to the Sun, thus weakened</note>, or afflicted,

---

it will give its "lesser years." But if it is in the 4th, 5th, or 11th house, and in its own house, exaltation, triplicity, or term, it will give the native its lesser years. But if it is retrograde or combust, or in its "fall" <note>The sign opposite its exaltation</note>, or afflicted, it will give months instead of lesser years. And if it is in the 6th, 12th, or 9th house, yet strong and "received" <note>A relationship where one planet is in the dignity of another</note> and free from malefics, it will give 6 months for its lesser years. But if in said places it is retrograde or combust or afflicted: it will give days or hours instead of its lesser years. For if the planets aspecting the **Alcocoden** are in the Ascendant, 10th, or 7th house, it will give its lesser years. For if they are in the 5th, 10th, or 4th, and also in their own houses, exaltations, triplicities, or terms, they shall offer their lesser years. But if they are retrograde, combust, or afflicted: they will give months instead of their lesser years. And if they are in the angles or "succedent" houses <note>The 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses</note>, and also retrograde, combust, or impeded, or in their fall as was said: they shall give days or hours instead of lesser years. Work therefore in this manner, adding with the benefics and subtracting with the malefics: and the benefics, through conjunction, sextile, or trine aspect, add to the years of the native. Malefics, however, through conjunction, square, or opposition, subtract. Abucussa <note>Likely a reference to Al-Qabisi (Alcabitius)</note> said: When you see the **Alcocoden** "pushing" <note>A technical term for the transfer of planetary influence</note> its power to another, and that planet is very strong, leave the **Alcocoden** and take that other planet instead of the **Alcocoden**; especially if it has received it, and has not returned its light and its order. This "pushing" is like one who promises to give something to another. "Reception" is like one who receives a gift. And he who "returns" is like one who cannot keep the gift he received. For if the **Alcocoden** pushes its power to a planet in the Ascendant or one having dignity in the **Hyleg**, assign the significance to it; provided that it does not push its power to another, but refers its power back to the **Alcocoden** itself. Certain wise men said that when a planet is in its own dignity and in the angles and is "occidental" <note>Setting after the Sun</note>, it will give its middle years. But if it is retrograde or afflicted, or "under the rays" <note>Close to the Sun, but not yet combust</note>, it will give its lesser years, unless it is separated from the Sun. And if it is in the Ascendant or the Midheaven, it will give its lesser years, but only if it has no dignity there. And if it is in the 4th or 7th house and has some power there, or is "oriental" <note>Rising before the Sun</note>, it will also give its lesser years. But if it has no dignity there, it shall offer its middle years. For the **Alcocoden** being in the 5th or 11th house and being oriental will give its middle years. If it is invisible, it shall offer its lesser years. For the **Alcocoden** being strong in the 9th house and having dignity there, if it is oriental, it will give its middle years. But if it is a pilgrim there, it will offer its lesser years. And say the same if it is in the 3rd house: except that the 3rd house is less strong than the 9th. And if the **Alcocoden** is in the 6th or 12th house and at the end of its strength or dignity, it will give its lesser years. But if it is weakened in said houses, it shall offer months; if retrograde or pilgrim, days; if combust, hours. When a malefic, from a square or opposite aspect, aspects the "significator of life" <note>The Hyleg</note>, it shall subtract its own years from the years given. And if said malefic aspects the significator by a trine or sextile aspect and from a good place, you shall add its lesser years to the years given. And if a benefic aspects it by any aspect, it shall subtract nothing from the years given, but shall always add. When the **Head of the Dragon** <term>Head of the Dragon: the North Node of the Moon</term> is with the **Alcocoden**, it shall subtract a fourth part of the years. Know that retrograde planets, due to the weakness of their significations, do neither good nor evil. For if benefics are in the opposite of their own houses, or in the opposite of malefics, they shall offer no good, but rather evil. And know that malefics are said to be worse when one aspects the other by an opposite aspect. When the **Alcocoden** has given certain years to the native and has verified them, a malefic cannot destroy those years. But if the **Alcocoden** is retrograde or afflicted, that malefic shall have those years, which will be contrary to the native. If malefics have dominion over the **Alcocoden** from the angles, and there is a space greater than their "body" <note>The physical diameter or orb of the planet</note> between them, they shall not subtract their years; rather they shall add. But the native shall be led into innumerable dangers. A benefic shall not add to the years of the **Alcocoden** from the angles unless it receives the **Alcocoden** and the **Alcocoden** receives it; otherwise there will be no addition or subtraction.

<meta>This page continues a technical astrological treatise, likely from a 15th or 16th-century printed edition of a work by an author like Alcabitius or Guido Bonatti. It focuses on the calculation of the length of life and the influence of planetary "terms" (divisions of zodiac signs) on a person's fate.</meta>

with an alliance. **When** indeed the Sun and Moon are unfortunate, they destroy and add misfortune in evil and destruction. **When** they are in their own dignity <note>Dignity refers to a planet being in a zodiac sign where it has natural strength</note>, or with their misfortunes, they will remove the evil from the misfortunes; but it is not to be said thus of **Mercury**. **When** he is with a misfortune, he will increase the evil and the misfortune. **When**, in any birth, they are surrounded by unfortunate luminaries, they will not be able to harm by the will of God, or at least they will harm very little. **Similarly**, when the misfortunes falling in a birth are the <term>Hyleg</term> <note>The planet or point that determines the life force and longevity of the newborn</note>, they will not harm in the revolution of the years. **Furthermore**, if the lord <term>Alcocoden</term> <note>The planet that determines the number of years a person will live, based on the Hyleg's position</note> is joined to the Sun in the birth, he adds his "lesser years." The **Head** of the Dragon <note>The North Node of the Moon, generally considered auspicious</note> neither adds nor diminishes. The **Head** of the Dragon, however, strengthens the Alcocoden. The **Tail** <note>The South Node of the Moon, generally considered malefic</note> weakens it and brings sickness to the native. **Thabit said:** <note>Likely referring to Thabit ibn Qurra, a famous 9th-century astronomer/astrologer</note> **When** the Alcocoden is in the same degree with the Head of the Dragon, it adds a fourth part of its years to the Alcocoden; but if it is with the Tail, it diminishes a fourth part from the Alcocoden. **And** if a planet in the birth, being stronger, reaches a better degree than it was at birth, it will direct and impel the native from weakness to strength and sublimity. **But** if it reaches a worse degree, it will do the opposite. **When** in the birth of any son of a king or prince the disposition of the heavens is evil, he will be unfortunate in his kingdom or principality. **And** this significance will be stronger if the planets from which the misfortune comes are in their ascension <note>Rising in the sky</note> and are mutual enemies, namely the "unfortunating" and the "unfortunate." **For** in that case, accidents of habits and place will happen to the native. **When** with this the misfortune is strong and that degree which is "unfortunated" is in a weak place, and the lord of its house does not behold it, nor does a fortune <note>A "fortune" is a beneficial planet like Jupiter or Venus</note> nor the fortunes apply to it by conjunction or aspect. **Look** in the birth of one whose life is short at the descension of the planets and their misfortune, as well as at the Sun. The **Moon** ascending, the Part of Fortune, and the degree of conjunction or prevention preceding the birth, and the degree of these significations is bound with a misfortune. **For** then the native shall die and his years shall end. **You** shall look at this regarding the weakness of the misfortune, because in whatever way it may be, whether falling, retrograde, or combust <note>Too close to the Sun, thus weakened</note>, and it has bound with it any of the aforesaid significations: it will kill the native, especially when his planets are unfortunate and the misfortunes are in the angles <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a chart, where planets are most active</note>. **For** if the Moon is in the 4th house or opposite its own place, it will kill the native. **For** if the Sun at the time of birth is in a "burning" house and a misfortune existing in an angle beholds it: the native will die at that hour when the Sun reaches the said misfortune. **When** the lord Alcocoden at the time of birth is unfortunate or in a weak place, and reaches the fourth aspect opposite its place, it will kill the native. **When** the native does not have an Alcocoden, look at the Part of Fortune and its lord, in which sign they fall: and take the degree of ascension of that sign in which the Part of Fortune is, according to the climate or latitude of that city in which he was born: and add upon this the circuit or revolution of the lord of the Part of Fortune: **And** say that the native shall live for as many months, days, or years according to the number of those degrees: or according to what you find in the years of the planets: for when the birth is unfortunate and the planets are unfortunate, and the lord of the Part of Fortune is unfortunate, **then** the native will live according to the aforesaid number for that many hours. **But** if the said planets are in the 10th house: the native will not complete a full year, unless God wills it. **Know** that when the ascending sign is at the 29th degree: the <term>Athazir</term> <note>A method of directing or progressing a point in the chart to predict timing</note> is of that 29th degree, and it is better from that if there is a fortune in the 2nd sign: because the lord of the 2nd will be a participant; since the said ascendant in that year and the following year will be alone in the following without the participation of the first.

### Concerning the years of the planets: which the wise use in the partitioning of the birth. Chapter 13.

The wise have taken the circuits of the planets, namely **Saturn, Jupiter, Mars,** and **Venus**, and the **Numbers** according to their terms through the 12 signs: and from the conjunction of **Mercury's** terms with each other, they made the "greater years" of the planets. **In** the Sun and Moon, however, they took their <term>Firdaria</term> <note>A system of planetary periods or "time lords" governing blocks of a person's life</note>. **And** because the years of the Sun's firdaria are 10, they multiplied them by 12 and arrived at 120 years: which they called the greater years of the Sun. **Similarly**, the years of the Moon's firdaria are 9, which being multiplied by 12 will reach 108 years. Therefore, the greater years of the Moon will be 108. **But** they took the "middle years" of the Sun and Moon by the mediation of their greater years: and the addition of their "lesser years" and the mediation of the whole aggregate. **For** the greater years of the Sun are, as was said, 120, which being halved reach 60; if we add the lesser years of the Sun, which are 19, they reach 79; if we halve these, they reach the middle years of the Sun, which are 39 and a half. The **cause** why the lesser years of the Sun are 19 is that from the beginning of Aries to the degree in which it is exalted are 19 degrees. **Therefore** the ancient wise men made their lesser years according to the quantity of these degrees. **And** because from the appearance of the Moon after its conjunction with the Sun until it is joined to it again are 25 days, they said that the lesser years of the Moon are 25. The **years** of **Mercury** are 20, because from the beginning of its hiding under the rays of the Sun until its appearance are 20 days. **But** the lesser years of **Saturn** are 30, because in 30 years it completes the circuit of its circle. **Similarly**, the lesser years of **Jupiter** are 12, because in 12 years it completes the circuit of its circle. The **years** of **Mars** are 15, because in that many months it makes a circle from the house of its diurnal sign, which is **Aries**, to the sign of its nocturnal sign, which is **Scorpio**. **But** the lesser years of **Venus** are eight, because in eight months it completes its circle. The **middle years** of the five aforesaid planets, namely **Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Venus,** and **Mercury**, are obtained thus: **Take** their lesser or small years: **And** add them with their greater years, and then subtract half; what remains after the subtraction will be the middle years.

### Concerning the science of the divisors or the partitioning of the years of the native. Chapter 14.

**When** you wish to have the divisor of the years in a birth, see of which planet the term of the ascending degree shall be, because that planet will be the divisor, giving one year to each degree. **And** when the term of the ascending degree has passed, look to the following term. **For** the lord of the said following term will be the divisor. **And** do thus from term to term until the life of the native is completed. **Look** therefore at the planets beholding the lord of the term, and combine their significations with the significations of the lord of the term. **For** if the lord of the term and the planets beholding it are fortunes, their significations will be for good; if misfortunes, for evil. **And** if the lord of the term is a misfortune and a fortune beholds that lord, mix their significations together and judge according to this. **An example** of this: **If Mercury** in any birth is the lord of the term of the ascending degree, and **Jupiter** beholds it with a square aspect, and **Venus** in a trine; while **Saturn** and **Mars** are in opposition: **Mercury**, since he is the lord of the term, will give the native knowledge and good intellect. **For** the aspect of **Jupiter** will be for him ornament and great status among other men; and the native will have the friendship of wise men and ecclesiastical prelates, as well as profit from them. **Similarly**, the aspect of **Venus** to **Mercury** will give the native beauty and good friendship with men, and joy with his wife and children. The **aspect** of **Saturn**, which signifies through the planet of pain, weeping, and sadness, means that sometimes one of his children will die, or that pains and sadness will invade him on account of his wife, money, or the melancholy of profit. **But** the aspect of **Mars** to **Mercury** signifies hot illnesses and evil through fire or the infusion of blood, or fear of death, and perhaps that he will fall from his honor and be deprived of his status. **And** if none of the planets behold the lord of the term, the lord of the term himself will be the sole disposer and divisor. **We must**, however, consider the manner of the native and his age, and in what time of life the lord of the term disposes him, and according to the quantity and condition of the planets in the hour of birth, as the wise have said to judge. **For** the wise have said that when **Mercury** is the lord of the term and has dominion over the ascending degree, he disposes knowledge, writing, and trade in the years attributed to his disposition; and his disposition will be from the hour of the native's birth until the years corresponding to the degrees of the term that are passed. **If**, for example's sake, there are still three degrees of the term of **Mercury** to pass, we should not judge because of this that the native will abound in knowledge in those three years, or that he will involve himself in science or trade, or that he will have familiarity with princes. **Since** because of his age it is not possible for him. **But** we will say of the native that in a short time he will speak and will move lightly, and that he will be intelligent, of good appearance, and subtle. **In the same way** we will speak of the disposition of **Venus**, which brings women as wives or expects to lie with them, when his age is repugnant to this. **But** we will say that he will be beautiful and well-nourished, pious, and of sweet appearance, and so with the other planets.

For if they cast their rays in strength upon the first degree or the third, and so on for the others, say that illnesses will invade him in the first or second year, and so on for the others according to the projection of the rays of the "infortunes" <note>Malefic planets, usually Saturn and Mars.</note> to the degree of the term. ¶ And I will give you a likeness or example in the arrangement of the terms and the projection of rays to the terms. ¶ Let the Ascendant be the 2nd degree of Gemini, and what remains of the term is 5 degrees. and you shall judge by the planets of the terms and the planets that rule over them. ¶ For when Jupiter disposes his power in the term of the newborn, the newborn will be well-fortuned, of good encounters, and of a good life, and beloved by his relatives. ¶ And if Saturn should cast his rays upon any degree of the said term, the newborn in the first or second year (and so on for the other numbers of that degree) will incur cold pains, or will have some impediment from a fall or ruin, or will suffer weeping and anguish on account of friends or relatives. ¶ And when the disposition reaches Mars, the newborn will travel, undertake many and strange labors, will have friendship with kings, will seize the goods of others, and will suffer many illnesses from which he will incur the fear of death. ¶ And when the disposition reaches Saturn, the newborn will encounter evil labor and illness; fevers and cold will invade him, and his body will be weakened; nor will the things started by him be completed, and he will be very solitary. ¶ But if he should have a wife or offspring, he will incur pain and sadness on their account. ¶ Therefore, when the terms of the sign of Gemini are completed, pass to the following sign, doing exactly with its terms as you did with the terms of the sign of Gemini; and so it is to be done from sign to sign and from term to term until the end of the life of the newborn.

### On the character of the newborn and his nature. Chapter 15.

¶ Know that the nature and character of the newborn are distinguished according to the significations and natures of the 7 planets in their forms and habits. ¶ For **Saturn**, by its own nature, signifies laziness, foolishness, foulness, fear, servitude, injury, lying, sadness, and an evil will of the heart. ¶ **Jupiter**, by its own nature, signifies law, faith, science, humility, shame <note>In the sense of modesty or bashfulness.</note>, fidelity, liberty, patience, sense, sobriety, and a clear face. ¶ **Mars**, by its own nature, signifies wrath, nausea <note>Likely meaning "loathing" or "disgust" in this context.</note>, violence, prodigality, heat, theft, shamelessness, and joy. ¶ The **Sun**, by its own nature, signifies strength, sublimity, probity, and great power. ¶ **Venus**, by its own nature, signifies humility, piety, good will, love of the heart, joy, and good character—except that the newborn will be considered effeminate and unbridled. ¶ **Mercury**, by its own nature, signifies trade, speech, the recitation of histories, intellect, speed of speech, grammar, logic, rhetoric, arithmetic, and astrology. ¶ The **Moon**, by its own nature, signifies a heavy gait, weakness, misery, journeys, whisperings, and the meddling of evil. ¶ And these significations of the planets will be strong or weak according to the strength or weakness of the planets in themselves and in their places. ¶ Therefore, when any of the planets is in the Ascendant or the Midheaven, it will give the newborn character and form according to its strength; but if an inimical planet should behold it <note>i.e., form an astrological aspect.</note>, it will change the given character according to the strength or weakness of that beholding planet. ¶ But if the said planets are not inimical to each other by aspect, then the character given by the first planet will be strengthened. So that if the first planet gave the newborn modesty, he will be so modest that he will hardly dare to speak; and so it is to be judged concerning the other significations of character signified by the planets. ¶ But when the said planets are inimical to each other by aspect or by nature, one renders its signification over the other, and one changes the character and properties of the other, as you can see here: for in the birth of one who is without fear or bold, you will find Mars in the Ascendant or in another strong place. ¶ And if the Moon or the Lord of the Ascendant should behold it and be in a strong place so that it has some dignity there, the newborn will be a lord and a master of soldiers, of great boldness, and will appear of great virtues; his boldness coming from the heart, he will desire to kill with his hand, he will love violence and infamy, he will be a traitor of a hard and ferocious heart, walking with a wide and long stride. ¶ And if Saturn should behold him, it will give him a heart of evil thoughts, fear, laziness, misery, heaviness of heart, coarseness, and sadness in his affairs. ¶ And if Jupiter should behold Mars, it will break his wrath and diminish his fury, and make him descend; it will restrain his levity and make him profit more by peace than by war, and will refrain his fury so that he will not kill anyone with his own hand, and will make him turn back from places where he might be captured or killed. ¶ And if the Sun should behold Mars, it will give him the strength of lordship and mastery over men and soldiers, as well as beauty of form, military skill, and intellect. ¶ And if Venus should behold Mars, the newborn will be a great lover of women and will have womanly habits, and will expose himself and his soul to evil and death on account of women and their jealousy; for he will be jealous beyond measure. ¶ And if Mercury should behold Mars, the newborn will be fearful, solitary, and one who will please others very little, nor will others please him; they will be of a foolish gait and much meditated; it is said he will not know what he wants to do and wants to do what he cannot. ¶ The **Beibenie** stars <note>original: "Stelle Beibenie" - Fixed stars of primary importance in medieval astrology, from the Persian *biyābānī*.</note> will also bring forth their significations and properties for the newborn; when they are with the Sun and the other planets, they themselves bring forth great and sudden fortune according to their nature. ¶ When a Beibenie star which is in the 2nd degree of Taurus of southern latitude and of the 2nd magnitude, and being of the nature of Mars, is in the Ascendant or Midheaven in a nocturnal birth, the newborn will be a soldier and a lord and an organizer of wars, and a man of high magnitude near to death, who will disdain to be under anyone. ¶ But if the birth is diurnal, the newborn will be of hard intellect, of evil gain, and one who acquires disgrace. ¶ And if in the place of the said Beibenie star there is any star of the nature of Jupiter and Mars, the newborn will be a master of soldiers and will go with many banners; he will be elevated in lordship and high in his place, of great fame, powerful in affairs and fortunate in war, and a wise man; he will have power in far-off lands, will be strong among citizens and lands, will love truth, and will be praised by many. ¶ Mars being in its own house in a nocturnal birth signifies that the newborn will be a lord and will have others under him; he will be a good soldier and fortunate in death and in wars, always conquering and wise in battles. ¶ And if it is in its own house in a diurnal birth, the newborn will be lazy in those things in which he ought to profit, weak, greedy, evil, a seizer of others' goods, a fornicator, loving death and evil, violent and insane. ¶ And if Mars is in the house of Saturn, the newborn will have a "fat liver" <note>Likely a reference to a specific temperament or physical condition.</note>, will be swift in his acts, will put an evil done to him in his heart and keep it for a long time, and will deliver his words hastily. ¶ And it has already been said that Mars being in the Midheaven and rejoicing in its place signifies that the newborn will be fortunate in war and death, and a handsome organizer of battles and wise in them. ¶ And if a "fortune" <note>A benefic planet like Jupiter or Venus.</note> should behold Mars thus disposed, the newborn will acquire utility and great fame from his wars. ¶ The Moon being in any of the angles or succedent houses and in any of her dignities, in aspect with Jupiter and Venus, signifies that the newborn will be a strong lord of wars, rejoicing and of a great heart, and patient of all things that happen to him. ¶ When a masculine planet is in a masculine sign: the newborn will be strong and complete in strength, of good sense, of good memory, wise in death, but prohibiting slaughter; a master of horses and apt in riding and arms. ¶ When the "Part of Boldness" <note>A calculated mathematical point in a horoscope.</note> falls in the house of Mars or in its term, and is in any of the angles (especially in the Midheaven), and also in aspect with Jupiter and Mars from a strong place: the newborn will be strong and famous among men, will be feared by others, will be a fortunate soldier in battles, and always a victor over his lines and his enemies.

### On him who has fear. Chapter 16.

¶ When Mars is "falling" <note>In a cadent house, a weak position.</note> in someone's birth, weak and unfortunate, not beholding the Ascendant, and Saturn beholds it, the newborn will be of a weak heart and timid, and also abhorring the place of perfection. ¶ Suppose when Venus is in...

### On the speed of the native toward wars and thefts. Chapter 17.

Mars being in the ascendant renders the native sharp toward discord and strife, a killer, and strong in madness. ¶ If Mercury is in the 4th house and appearing from the rays of the Sun, and also in aspect to Mars and Saturn: the native will be sharp and of swift change, always insane and meddlesome. ¶ For Mars, being in the 8th house and in its own sign in either a diurnal or nocturnal nativity, signifies that the native will be insane, and a lasting madman, and a foolish killer. ¶ If Mercury is in its own house: the native will be timorous and insane. ¶ If the Moon, increased in light, is in the house of Mars: the native will be sharp, wrathful, of a large liver <note>In medieval medicine, a "large liver" was associated with heat and intense passion or anger.</note>, seeking wars, and openly bringing reproaches against men. ¶ If the Moon in a nocturnal nativity applies to Mars and is increased in light: the native will be of great movement toward anger, seeking strife and discord without cause. ¶ If the Part of Fortune or its lord, or the lord of the house of life, is in the house of Mars, the native will be furious, always sad, of great madness, and slow to return to reason.

### On the humility of the native. Chapter 18.

When Mercury is under the rays of the sun and in a watery sign—because they lack a voice—and Mars looks upon it, the native will be humble and quiet, and grave in his actions. ¶ If Saturn and Mars look upon the Moon and the Sun (or either of them) from the midheaven, or the ascendant, or from the 7th or 4th house, the native will be humble and self-aware, provided they look upon the Sun in a diurnal nativity and the Moon in a nocturnal nativity, and the Sun or Moon is in the term of Fortune. ¶ If one of the Beibenian stars <note>term: Beibenijs (from Arabic 'al-biya-banni'). These are specific fixed stars considered to have significant astrological influence.</note> of the nature of Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn, being without great magnitude, is in the ascendant or midheaven, the native will be humble, of subtle wit, and of good change.

### On the shamelessness of the native. Chapter 19.

Know that when Mars looks upon Mercury from an opposite aspect, the native will be without shame. ¶ But if the Moon looks upon Mars from an opposite aspect, it will diminish his shamelessness and change his litigiousness. ¶ And if the Moon, diminished in light and being in a bad place, is with Mars, the native will be without shame. ¶ If Venus and Mercury are in the ascendant and in opposite aspect to Mars, the native will have little shame.

### On the modesty of the native. Chapter 20.

Jupiter being in the ascendant of any nativity without the aspect of Mars signifies that the native will be modest. ¶ If the Moon is in the ascendant and in the term of Saturn and in his face, while Mars is in the west and Jupiter looks upon the Moon, the native will be modest.

### On the beauty of the native's character. Chapter 21.

When the Moon, being in the midheaven, looks upon Saturn with a 4th aspect <note>A square aspect (90 degrees).</note> while Saturn is in the 2nd house, it is a sign that the native will be of beautiful character. ¶ The Sun being in the house of Saturn signifies that the native will be of a good soul. ¶ If Fortune looks upon the Sun, the native will have good [fortune] and good manners and joy. ¶ If Mercury is in the house of the Sun in a diurnal nativity, the native will be of good character, especially if he is safe from the aspect of misfortune. ¶ If Mercury is in the house of the Moon, fortunate and without the aspect of misfortune, the native will be of good character and a beautiful face. ¶ If the Sun and Moon are in the exaltation of Venus without the aspect of misfortune, the native will be of beautiful character, subtle wit, and a cheerful face, and also beloved by men.

### On the lying of the native. Chapter 22.

When Mars is with Mercury in his house, the native will be a liar and will utter lying words without reason or root, and will adorn his will with a lying tongue. ¶ If Mars, the lord of the angle of the earth <note>The 4th house (Imum Coeli).</note>, is in the 6th house, the native will be a liar. ¶ If the Sun and Venus are in the 7th house, the native will be full of lies. ¶ If Mars is in the 9th house, the native will bring forth lies and affirm them by swearing. ¶ If any of the Beibenian stars of the nature of Mercury and Venus are in the ascendant or midheaven: the native will be a liar and will confirm his lies by swearing, and will write false letters, and will compose things that men did not say or do; he will then be a boaster. ¶ If the Moon, separated from Mars, applies to Mercury and both are oriental: the native will be a liar as well as a master of fraud and testing. ¶ If Mars and Mercury, being in the same degree in any of the angles without the aspect of anyone, the native will be a liar and a toucher <note>Likely meaning a meddler or one who handles things dishonestly.</note> of those things he did not do, and will insert deceptive words into his speech.

### On the truthfulness of the native. Chapter 23.

When the Moon and Venus are in the midheaven, the native will be truthful and a fulfiller of promises. ¶ If the native is born with Sagittarius in the ascendant and the Moon in it, and in the 3rd or 6th aspect of Jupiter, it is a sign that the native will be truthful and fulfilling those things he promises. ¶ When Jupiter is in the ascendant and Mercury is in the 7th house under the rays, the native will be truthful. ¶ Mercury being in the ascendant or midheaven with Venus signifies that the native will be a truthful orator, fulfilling promises, and uttering the truth both for himself and against himself.

### On the religion of the native. Chapter 24.

Jupiter being in the 9th house <note>The 9th house traditionally governs religion, philosophy, and long journeys.</note> and in a diurnal nativity signifies that the native will be a worshiper of God, trusting in God, and contemplative, so that on account of the beautiful adornment he has in his law, he will be honored by kings and powers. ¶ If Saturn is in his triplicity, the native will be God-fearing and praying, [using] holy words, and sometimes angelic ones, and will frequently receive penance for his promises, weeping for his sins; and this will be stronger if Jupiter looks upon Saturn with reception in a good aspect. ¶ If the Moon is in the ascendant and in her exaltation, and also in the 3rd or 9th aspect of Jupiter, the native will be a contemplative orator and a lord of churches or a distributor to the poor. ¶ If the Sun is in the 3rd house or in a masculine sign, and also in a good aspect of Jupiter, it is a sign that the native will be an orator obedient to God, leaving all worldly things for the fear and love of God. ¶ If Venus is in the 3rd house and in her exaltation in a nocturnal nativity, it is a sign that the native will be established and firm in the love of God and His obedience. ¶ If Mercury, the Sun, and Venus are joined in the 9th house without the aspect of misfortune, the native will always have God before his eyes and will show Him to those to whom he wishes to do good. ¶ If Venus is in the 9th house and in her own house or her exaltation, the native will be a famous hermit, and this more strongly if Jupiter looks upon her with a good aspect. ¶ If Mercury is in the 9th house, fortunate without the aspect of misfortune and oriental, the native will be good and fortunate in his law. ¶ If the Moon is in the 9th house and in a feminine sign, and also in a good aspect of Jupiter in a nocturnal nativity, the native will be an orator and a hermit.

### On the hypocrisy of the native. Chapter 25.

When Jupiter is in a nocturnal nativity in the 3rd house with Mars and in a masculine sign, the native will be a hypocrite. ¶ If Venus, being a pilgrim <note>term: peregrina. A planet is "peregrine" when it has no essential dignity (house, exaltation, triplicity, term, or face) in its current position, often weakening its influence.</note>, is in the 9th house and a feminine sign, the native will want it said of him that he is good, but he will be a hypocrite seeking razors <note>A metaphor for sharp, cutting deceit.</note> to perform trickery. ¶ If the Part of Absence is in any of the angles and its lord looks upon a fortune without reception and is safe from misfortunes, the native wants men to speak well of him and will pretend to do good while doing evil. ¶ But if the said Part and its lord are strong and fortunate, free from misfortunes, the native will be of good words and secret will. ¶ And if the said Part is unfortunate and its lord free from misfortunes, the native will be better on the inside than the outside. ¶ And if both are in angles and free from misfortunes, the native will be firm in his appearance and little of a hypocrite. ¶ If, therefore, the lord of the said Part is received and looks upon its place, the native will be established, good, and fortunate. ¶ But if he is retrograde, the native will doubt in his faith and become of another faith. ¶ And if the lord of the said Part of Absence is in the 9th house, it signifies goodness of faith in the native, especially if he is received. ¶ For if they do not have reception in a conquered place, it portends contrariety on account of faith and the division of the world. ¶ If with this he is unfortunate, it signifies doubt in faith and loss because of this. ¶ And judge in the same way if he is in the 3rd house. ¶ But if he is thus in the 11th house, it signifies constancy in faith, and he will withdraw from secular things, and the more he serves, the more he will flee worldly things. ¶ The lord of the Part of Absence, free from misfortunes and looking upon its place, will prohibit the native from hypocrisy.

and will add to his goodness. If it [the planet] is received, that goodness will be joined with truth and righteousness. <note>Reception is an astrological concept where one planet is in a sign ruled by another.</note> But if it is unfortunate, it will destroy the innate goodness of the person born. If the Part of Absence is in falling houses, the goodness which the person appears to have will eventually be destroyed. But if the lords of the said parts are unfortunate, the goodness of the person, both in appearance and from within, will be considered as nothing. The Sun in the 3rd house, being in any of its dignities, signifies a total lack of faith. And if the Moon is in the 3rd house in a nighttime birth, the person will appear just: but he will show more of this outwardly than he has in his heart.

### On the Idolatry of the Person Born. Chapter 26.

**W**hen Jupiter is in the ascendant and the Moon is in the descendant and Mars regards Jupiter, the person born will worship the stars and powers. Where Venus appears under the rays <note>original: "sub radijs" - meaning too close to the sun to be seen</note> in the 2nd house, the person will be an idolater. Similarly, Saturn in the 3rd house with the Moon signifies the person will be an idolater and a teacher of the contrary [faith]. When the Moon is in the 9th house in a daytime birth and in a feminine sign, the person will be a despiser of God and his adversary. When Venus is besieged by Saturn and Mars in one sign, and the unfortunates <note>Mars and Saturn</note> regard the Moon and Mercury with a quartile or opposite aspect, the person will be a worshiper of many idols. Mars in the ascendant in opposition to Jupiter will show the person to be an idolater. If Mercury with Mars is in the house of Jupiter, the person will be like a hermit of idols. When Saturn is the lord of the ascendant, or lord of the house of the Sun, or of the Part of Fortune, or of the opposition or conjunction, and is in the 9th house and Jupiter regards him: the person will be an abbot or a high priest of idols. And if Mercury is in the house of Mars and Mars regards him or is in opposite aspect, the person will be a heretic. And similarly if the Sun, Moon, and Mercury are in a double-bodied sign <note>e.g., Gemini, Pisces</note> with the fortunates, if Mars shines there with a quartile or opposite aspect.

### On the Knowledge of the Person Born. Chapter 27.

**W**hen Saturn regards Jupiter with a trine aspect: the person will be wise, of good counsel and character, of sound thought, and skilled in all the secrets of nature: so that it will be said of him that he is a philosopher; he will be a narrator of the deeds of kings and will predict grave matters like the soothsayers <term>Auruspex: a diviner who interprets omens</term>. When Mercury is in his own house or exaltation and in a good figure and place, and Mars regards him with a good aspect, the person will be very wise in the course of the stars and grave in his affairs, and of profound thought; he will prefer many books, join them to himself, and speak wonderful things. When Mercury is with the Sun in its own house, the Sun being in its own face, the person will be a wise physician and a practitioner of physics. When Mercury is in the midheaven, direct and in any of its dignities, and the Moon regards it with a quartile or opposite aspect, and the Moon is in her own house or exaltation, the person will be wise, sensible, and an observer of the stars. When Mercury is fortunate in its own house, the person will be a lover of books and the science of the stars, and a commentator on those difficult matters. When Mercury, being in his house or exaltation, is in the ascendant, and Saturn is in the descendant, the person will investigate grave and difficult sciences and through this will have lordship and sublimity. When Mars regards Mercury in any of its dignities with a trine aspect and Mars is fortunate, the person will be a wise and experienced philosopher. When the Moon is in her house or exaltation or in the term of Mercury, the person will be wise in secret and obscure or hidden things. When Mercury is in the 3rd house with <unclear>...</unclear> and in its face, and he is also the lord of the ascendant and the Part of Fortune, the person will be a soothsayer and wise in secret matters. When Mercury, appearing from under the rays of the Sun, is in the said 3rd house and in a masculine sign, the person will be an augur <note>a priest who interprets the flight of birds</note> and will live by augury and have profit from it. But if Mars is oriental and regards Mercury so disposed, the person will have knowledge of books and be wise in them. When Mercury is in the 9th house without the aspect of Mars, the person will be an interpreter of dreams, and this more strongly if Saturn is in his house or exaltation. But if Venus is disposed toward Saturn as we have said, the person will likewise be an interpreter of dreams. When Mercury and the Sun are in the 9th house and in any of their dignities, the person will be wise and an interpreter of dreams and like a soothsayer. When Mercury is oriental and fortunate in the 9th house, the person will be an augur. When the Part of Fortune and the Part of Faith are in the 9th or 3rd house without the aspect of the fortunates and unfortunates, and regard them from the opposite of their house, the person will be a soothsayer and will unlock the secrets of obscurities. When Saturn and Mercury are in the 3rd house, being in their house or exaltation, the person will be wise in the stars and have profit from it. And say the same if Mercury appears under the rays in the 3rd house. And if Venus is a pilgrim <note>in a sign where she has no dignity</term> in the 3rd house and Saturn regards her from his house or exaltation with a good aspect, the person will be a necromancer: or will do wonderful things by art. He will be seen as a prophet and will bring about wonders, especially if Venus is in a feminine sign. When both Mercury and Saturn are fortunate and free in the ascendant or midheaven, the person will be a necromancer. When Mercury is in his house or exaltation and Saturn, while Mercury is unfortunate, regards him with a quartile or opposite aspect, the person will be a physician, but his science will not profit him: he will be miserable and poor. When Mercury is fortunate in the house of Saturn, the person will be wise in knowing secret and obscure things, talkative, rhetorical, an augur, and an interpreter of dreams. When Sagittarius or Pisces is the sign of the 3rd house and Saturn is in it, the person will be wise in good things. When Mercury with Venus is in the 4th house, the person will be a casuistic disputant and an inventor of words, and this more strongly if in a pilgrim sign. When the Sun is in the 9th house, the person will be a lawyer, a disputant in faith, and a praiser of God. When Jupiter in a daytime birth is the lord of the 3rd house and the sign of said house is masculine, the person will be wise. And if it is a nighttime birth, the person will be believed to be wise but will not be. If the Moon, separated from the Sun, joins Mercury in the 3rd house, the person will be a writer and wise in computation. When the Sun and Mercury or Jupiter are in the 9th or 3rd house and in Leo, the person will be of clear intellect and an organizer of things. When Mercury is fortunate in the midheaven, the person will be subtle and of quick genius. And similarly if Mercury is in the house of Mars. When the Moon is with Saturn in its orientality, the person will be intelligent but lazy and soft. When the Moon is separated from Venus and joined to Mercury and both are occidental, the person will be subtle and sharp. When Jupiter receives Mars, or regards him with a quartile aspect, the person will be of good counsel and intellect. When the Sun is in the house of Jupiter or in its 3rd or 6th aspect, the person will be of good counsel, intellect, and profound science. But if Mercury is with him, the person will be skilled in law and wise in judgments. When Mercury is fortunate in the house of the Sun in a daytime birth, the person will be sharp and subtle.

### On the Memory of the Person Born. Chapter 28.

**W**hen the Sun is in the house of Mars and Jupiter regards it with a good aspect, the person will be of good memory. When Venus and Mars with the Moon are well-fortuned, the person will be mindful and a deceiver in genius. When Mercury and Mars are fortunate and regard the Moon, the person will beautifully relate those things which he knows. When Mercury is in the house of the Sun and Jupiter regards him with a good aspect, the person will be intelligent and mindful and of quick intellect. When Jupiter is joined with him, the person will be a cleric or a judge among clerics.

### On the Bad Intellect of the Person Born. Chapter 29.

**W**hen Mercury is in the 2nd house and under the rays of the Sun, and the Moon is diminished in light and unfortunate, the person will be of bad intellect, weak in sense, not receiving instruction. When Mercury is in the 4th house and is the lord of the Part of Absence, the person will be of bad intellect, of heavy speech, of little science and sense. When the Sun is in the house of the Moon and she is unfortunate under the earth and diminished in light: the person will be of hard and bad intellect: like a donkey, of much error, weak counsel, and a thick brain. When Mercury and Saturn are unfortunate in the ascendant and Jupiter is retrograde and a pilgrim and regards them with an opposite aspect, the person will be of hard and bad intellect, knowing and understanding little.

### On the Good and Quick Intellect of the Person Born. Chapter 30.

**W**hen Jupiter regards a fortunate Mercury with a 3rd aspect, the person will be sensible, of good speech, and of quick intellect. When Mercury is in his own house and
