CONCERNING those born who will receive evil from their friends. Chapter 205.

**W**hen the sign of the 3rd house is Taurus or Libra, and an ill-fortune <note>a malefic planet like Saturn or Mars</note> being the lord of the 11th house aspects that sign: the native will follow evil and detriment from his friends. When Saturn is in the 3rd house: the native will receive vehement evil. When the lord of the 3rd house is in the 6th house: the native will have detriment from friends or kinsmen. When the lord of the 12th house is in the 12th house, and the Part of Friends <note>a calculated mathematical point in the zodiac</note> is in the 12th house: the native will receive grievances and labors from friends. When Virgo is in the house of friends: the native will be of little friendship, so much so that friends will not care for his friendship. And if an ill-fortune is there and the lord of the Ascendant is afflicted: the native will incur evil and detriment by occasion of his friends.
Chapter on the 12th house, which is the significator of enemies.

**L**ook for the enemies of the Native at the 12th house and its lord, and at the Part of Enemies and its lord; at Saturn and at any planet existing in the 12th house; likewise at the planet which has the greatest strength and dominion in said places. And it should be known that if in the house of enemies there is a sign, and its lord, as well as the Part of Enemies and its lord, are in a common sign <note>Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces</note>: the native will have many enemies. When many planets are in the house of enemies, the native's enemies will be many, and their status will be in weakness or strength according to the state of said planets. When Gemini is the sign of the house of enemies: the native's enemies will be many and weak, so that they cannot harm him. When Cancer is the sign of the house of enemies: the native will humble himself toward his enemies and will fear them much. And if Leo is the sign of said house: the native will be stronger, will act as an enemy, and make them fear him much. And if Libra is the sign of said house: the native will have many enemies, and no one will be an enemy to him who does not also litigate with him. When Pisces is the sign of said house: the native will have enemies without fault, and will be envious of all. When the planets signifying the lord of the Ascendant and the planet which has the greatest dominion in the nativity aspect [the 12th house], the native's enemies and adversaries will multiply, and their status will be in weakness or strength according to the state of said planets. When a planet in the house of enemies is the lord of the house of brothers: the greater part of the native's enemies will be from his brothers or kinsmen or from the men of his house. And if it is the lord of the house of fathers: parents will be enemies to him; and say the same of other houses according to their nature and signification. For if the lord of the house of enemies is in that same house: the native will be envious of himself, and will have few enemies who do not reveal their enmity against him, nor harm him openly; but their enmity will be hidden. When the lord of the house of enemies is in the Ascendant: the native will be laborious and have many enemies who will bring him anxieties in the beginning of his life. And if it is in the 2nd house: the native will be of evil works and of evil virtue and status, and many evil words and lies will be said of him. And if it is in the 8th house: the native will have few enemies and will inherit much substance from them. And if it is in the 9th house: his brothers will receive grievances from enemies. But if Saturn takes hold there, he will have troubles in it and will be of an evil law <note>meaning a bad religion or lack of morals</note>. When the lord of the Ascendant is in the 12th house: the native will be an enemy to himself and will show himself more than another. When the Part of Enemies is free from ill-fortunes and is in any angle, and its lord likewise: the native will be an enemy to powerful men and those stronger than himself, and labor and evil will happen to him; and this signification will be stronger if the lord of the Ascendant is afflicted. When the Part of Enemies is in the 3rd, 5th, 9th, or 11th house: the native's enemies will have no fortune over him, and he will have few enemies. And this signification will be stronger if said Part is in the 3rd or 9th house. For then the native's enemies will be religious or of little offense. And if with this the said Part is afflicted: the native's enemies will be enchanters or will speak with demons. For if the Part of Enemies is in the 11th house: the native's friends will become his enemies. And if it is in the 5th house: his children will be enemies. And if it is in the 2nd, 6th, 8th, or 12th house: the native will have few enemies and they will not harm him, provided the lord of the Part is an ill-fortune and afflicts the lord of the Ascendant and the said Part. And it should be known that the enmity will be of the nature of that house in which the said Part is: for if it is in the 1st house <note>The text says 1st, though the logic follows the 2nd</note> it will be enmity for the sake of money. If in the 6th, for the sake of servants; if in the 8th, for the sake of the inheritances of the dead. And if in the 12th house, for the sake of envy. When the lords of the conjunction or prevention <note>New or Full Moon</note> preceding the nativity are in the 12th house, the native will die suddenly. When Saturn or Mars, being lord of the 12th house, is in the Ascendant: the native will have a great desire for hunting and will perhaps be killed by a wild beast or <unclear>tarte</unclear>. When the Head of the Dragon <note>the North Node of the Moon</note>, the Sun, Moon, and Mercury are in the 12th house, they signify great and strong infirmities, or the breaking of bones, or blindness, or burning, and that the native will die an evil death, or be killed malignantly by the hands of his enemies, especially if Saturn is with them in the 12th house.
Concerning those born who will have their enemies in their power. Chapter 206.

**W**hen Mercury is strong and fortunate in its own place: the native will be able to resist his enemies and subjugate them. When Mars and Venus are in the West or in the angle of the earth <note>the 4th house</note>: the native will prevail over his enemies and they will humble themselves and come to his feet. When the lord of the 12th house is combust <note>too close to the sun</note> or impeded, or falling, or peregrine, or afflicted by any ill-fortune: the native's enemies will be few and will come to mercy, and fear will enter upon them. When the lord of the Ascendant does not aspect the 12th house: the native will have few enemies. When the planet which has the greatest power in the Ascendant overcomes the planet joining the testimonies of the signs of the enemies: the native will prevail over his enemies, and they will be under his feet and he shall have what he wants from them. When Venus aspects neither of these planets: the native will escape from his enemies and live in leisure and peace without fear. For if any ill-fortune is in the house of enemies and the lord of the house of enemies is in an evil place afflicted by some ill-fortune: the native will have what he desires regarding his enemies. When a fortune <note>Jupiter or Venus</note> being in any of its dignities is in the 12th house: the native's enemies will be good and just, and he will escape from them unharmed. And if an ill-fortune is there, the native's enemies will be evil. When the lord of the house of the Part of Enemies applies to the lord of the Ascendant with a good aspect: the native's enemies will become friends with the native and humble themselves before him. If God wills, etc.

---

-> Here ends the Book of Nativities of Albubather: the great Al- <-
-> Khasib's son, with the praise of Almighty God. <-
-> Translated from Arabic into Latin at Padua, 1218. <-

---

-> A poem by Antonius Bonus Cataneus of Brescia to <-
-> Antonius Laurus of Padua. <-

> You who happily approach the accustomed chariots of Phoebus <note>the Sun</note> and the dwellings of the sisters <note>the Muses</note>, your temples bound with green Laurel: Grant to the youth—you who traverse the joyful fields by the divine power of Pallas <note>Athena</note>—help, you to whom you minister your deeds. Here is the Aonian grove; the Myrtle is green; the unwed Laurel grows. No soft lime trees, no elms clothed with vines rise here; no fragile hazels or slender tamarisks. Here the Laurels are fragrant, and the myrtle planted with various flowers. Here the Muses reside; captivated, you cling to them. Run, O Fates, concordant with your stable spindle.
-> Farewell. <-

---

### An epistle of the same to the reader.

> Great gifts indeed we owe to our Laurus <note>Antonius Laurus, the editor/patron</note>, by whose help the glory of Albubather shines forth. He brought these pious dogmas, which had been in rare papers. These are the sayings noted by an eminent man. Now, reader, if anyone holds your name as a gift, he will often owe much to your study. Now the studious cohort will rejoice, and give you praise. May your name be eternal above the stars. If, however, any page should be without worthy cultivation, O candid reader, it will be the fault of another. And now farewell; and may you, most mild one, have our praises. O Laurus, the hope and glory of the men of Antenor <note>the people of Padua, founded by the mythical Antenor</note>.

--- Page 53 ---
<meta>This page serves as the frontispiece and introductory dedication for the "Centiloquium" (One Hundred Sayings) attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, a legendary Hellenistic figure associated with magic and alchemy. It follows the previous astrological works, establishing the authority of the author and the editor, Antonius Laurus.</meta>

<page-num>21</page-num>
<header>The One Hundred Sayings of the Divine Hermes.</header>

# The One Hundred Sayings of the Divine Hermes.

## By Hilarius of Mantua.
## In Praise of Hermes, an Epigram.

> I am that Hermes, Thrice-Greatest, dweller of the mighty Nile:
> Who is worshipped with honor by the Egyptian people.
> With a skillful mind, I taught what certain future events would come:
> Or what the stars might bring through their own motions.
> I was once foul and shivering, with moths gnawing at my papyrus:
> Unknown for a long time, and found only rarely.
> At last, the diligent care of Antonius Laurus has brought me
> Out of the darkness; he who honors the stars.

## Another Epigram by the same Hilarius.

> Hermes is Mercury, who discovered the arts of all things:
> And who first taught how to speak words in order.
> Calling each thing by name: he first established the elements
> And ordained that the sacred powers of the gods be worshipped.
> He knew the courses of the stars: the moderator of voices:
> The interpreter of words: he also discovered numbers:
> Let learned youths hold him, and let the old desire him:
> Hermes, the author, produced this divine work.

---

> Almansor <note>A reference to the 9th-century Persian astrologer Al-Mansur</note> wrote thrice-fifty precepts of the heavens:
> In which one may know what the stars intend.

# Antonius Laurus to Caesar.
**Interpreter of the Law: from a small beginning: Count of the Imperial Palace, etc. To the musical and most upright Lord, Domenico, a nobleman of Brescia, son of the most illustrious monarch of both laws, Lord Laurentius Calcagno: he wishes him prosperous years, whether of the Eagle, the Phoenix, or Nestor <note>Metaphors for extreme longevity</note>.**

<margin>
<note>The decorative initial 'Q' marks the beginning of the formal dedication, a common feature in Renaissance printing to signal a new section of text.</note>
</margin>

Since I have completed the work of Albubathre <note>Abu Bakr al-Hasan, a 9th-century Persian astrologer</note>: if through him I understand that I have partially satisfied those studious of such doctrine—so that I might, to the best of my ability, comply with the excellence of many of my friends who faithfully urged this work upon me (though it be unequal to my talents)—I promised to bring to light another brief and distinguished work very soon, according to the saying: *Anyone can be rich in promises.* But because some people altogether attempt to refuse to admit Solon, and so many philosophers, Ptolemy, and such great astronomers as judges or witnesses in this matter—as if they were suspect—even though the proof of the truth is clearer than the midday light; and indeed, they do so unjustly. Does not experience, the mistress of things, create art? For it is a rule in civil law that experience must be had in every single thing, and one must believe a person skilled in their art. Every craft receives increase through continuous exercise. We also read that those who know should be consulted. For this reason, the Grammarians believe Priscian; the Rhetoricians believe Tully <note>Cicero</note>; the Dialecticians believe Aristotle; the Philosophers believe Socrates and Plato; the Physicians believe Avicenna, Hippocrates, and Galen; the Geometricians believe Euclid; the Lawyers believe Justinian; the Canonists believe Gratian. Thus, in all things in this noble science of Astronomy, I judge it right that Ptolemy and the other authors should not be undeservedly believed. For a doctor is believed regarding his medicine; a smith regarding his forge; a teacher regarding his student; a Master of Soldiers regarding his soldier; an advocate regarding the case in which he pleads; a surveyor regarding his office; a notary regarding his duty; and a traveler regarding his path. A confessor is believed regarding a confession. And generally, everyone is to be believed who is experienced in his own art. Returning then to our subject: according to the witness of Michael Scot <note>A 13th-century mathematician and scholar</note>, the arts of writing are seven, according to the planets which are attributed to them here: Grammar to the Moon; Dialectic to Mercury; Rhetoric to Venus; Arithmetic to the Sun; Music to Mars; Astronomy to Jupiter; Geometry to Saturn. Just as in minerals: Lead to Saturn; Tin to Jupiter; Iron to Mars; Gold to the Sun; Copper to Venus; Quicksilver to Mercury; Pure Silver to the Moon. And so, infinite things could be said regarding each in the number seven. Whence also that saying of Persius in his 5th Satire: *There are a thousand kinds of men, and diverse uses of things. Each has his own inclination, nor is life lived by a single wish.* We, therefore, have gathered these flowers, judgments, conclusions, aphorisms, or propositions, at least for our friends; lest for lack of books they cite the saying to their kin: *They do not easily emerge, whose virtues are blocked by narrow circumstances at home.* To these same people I say: please accept these delights with which you frequently deigned to receive me so splendidly and elegantly in your own homes in Brescia with innate kindness; from whom I never approached so learned that I did not depart more learned. Come now, sweetest Antonius, either conquer or equal the Laurel <note>A pun on his name, Laurus</note> with your mutual love, as you do. Venice, the Ides of June, 1492.
<sig>Francis of Tri. 4. Fame.</sig>

->And that one of Lon who did a much better work,<- ->If only the aphorisms were well understood.<-

To whom; what kind; to whom also; who; and from where: the first letters of each notable point show these beforehand.

To the Lord Manfred, the renowned King of Sicily, Stephen of Messina translated these flowers from the secrets of the Astrology of the divine Hermes.

## Here begins the Book of the Hundred Aphorisms of Hermes.

<margin>Note 1.</margin>

**H**ermes said: The Sun & Moon, after God, are the life of all living things. Indeed, the births of many do not have a <term>hyleg</term> <note>original: "hyleg", the life-giving planet</note>; but because the Sun and Moon behold their ascendant out of love, their life may be prolonged further while that same [ascendant] remains free. All diurnal births are strengthened by the Sun, when it is configured with the benevolents. Nocturnal [births], however, by the Moon, when she is beheld by the benevolents. And if this should not be...

<summary>This page concludes the alphabetical index of astrological topics and begins the "Centiloquium," a famous collection of 100 astrological aphorisms attributed to the legendary sage Hermes Trismegistus. The text introduces the vital roles of the Sun and Moon in determining the lifespan and strength of a newborn's chart.</summary>
<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, Centiloquium, hyleg, astrology index, planetary aspects, Sun and Moon, nocturnal birth, diurnal birth, medical astrology</keywords>

--- Page 55 ---
<meta>This page continues a series of astrological and medical aphorisms, likely from a Renaissance-era collection such as the Centiloquium. The text focuses on the influence of planets in specific houses and signs, providing rules for both natal astrology (birth charts) and horary astrology (answering specific questions).</meta>

# Page 45

If, however, there are good planets in the angles <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, considered the most powerful positions in a chart</note>: then the birth is fortunate. M. 2.

When Mars is the lord of the ascendant in the 10th house, he owes the native dignity and power; which he will use with injustice and cruelty; and this is truly called a misfortune rather than a fortune. M. 3.

Jupiter, when configured with malefic planets <note>Usually Saturn or Mars</note>, changes their malice into good: Venus cannot do this unless she is aided by Jupiter. And therefore, in increasing good and in prohibiting evil, Jupiter is considered much better than Venus. 4.

The Astrologer cannot make a combination of the significations of the stars before he knows their friendships and enmities. Their friendship and enmity is threefold: one according to nature; another according to their houses; and the third according to their aspects. 5.

Venus is opposed to Mercury. This one <note>Mercury</note> embraces speeches and disciplines; that one <note>Venus</note> truly embraces pleasures and delights. Similarly, Jupiter is opposed to Mars. This one <note>Jupiter</note> wants mercy and justice; that one <note>Mars</note> truly wants impiety and cruelty. 6.

For the deeds of great princes, make the Sun the significator, or one of the superior planets <note>Saturn, Jupiter, or Mars</note>. For scribes and rustics, use the inferior planets: and especially the Moon. 7.

An aspect cannot diminish the significance of a conjunction. But a conjunction diminishes the significance of an aspect. For a conjunction is stronger than an aspect. 8.

Do not determine or choose anything while Scorpio is in the ascendant: nor when the angles are oblique; or if Mars be in them: for a false outcome of the definition will happen, especially because Scorpio is a sign of falsehood. 9.

A benevolent planet <term>benivolus</term> <note>A planet like Jupiter or Venus that brings good fortune</note> brings evil from the 6th or 12th place when it is hindered there by malefic planets. 10.

Rumors told while the Moon is in the first face <note>The first ten degrees</note> of Scorpio are lying and fabricated. 11.

Judgments of astrologers sometimes turn out vain: either because of an error of the instruments; or because of the ignorance of the petitioner; or when the Sun is near the degree of the midheaven; or when the figures that grant or deny a thing are equal. 12.

While the Moon is southern and descending in Scorpio or Pisces, do not begin to build. For such a building will quickly fall down. 13.

It is necessary for Mercury to be strong in the locations of persons and by the configurations of other stars in births so that the native may have dignity. For Mercury by himself is a weak planet. M. 14.

In signs of two bodies <note>Mutable signs like Gemini or Pisces</note>, victory is good, but defeat is bad: for both are doubled. 15.

Do not define anything before you know the intention of the seeker. For many do not know how to ask: nor can they express what they intend. 16.

When you are asked about a father, look to the 4th house; about a brother, the 3rd; about a son, the 5th; about a wife, the 7th. If, however, you are asked for a sick person: look to no other than the ascendant. 17.

When the Moon comes to the squares of the benevolent or the malefic planets, both the hindrance and the help will be doubtful: and it is to be feared lest the malefics hinder and the benevolents cannot help. 18.

In the beginning of journeys and returns, let not the Moon be in the ascendant, or in the 4th, or in the 9th, even if she is not hindered. In the entry of a city, let her not be in the ascendant, or the 2nd, or the 4th. Similarly, not in the entry of a house. 19.

The accidents of men are foreknown in three ways: either from their own birth; or from that of the first-born son; or from an inquiry known with affection and care. 20.

Every beginning made when the Moon is joined to a retrograde planet is quickly destroyed: and it comes to a worse state when she is hindered. 21.

The being of kings and princes is taken from Saturn and the Sun and from the planet existing in the 10th house. The helpers of the king from the 11th. And the helpers of the rustics from the 2nd place. 22.

The departure of a king or prince for journeys is utterly disapproved when Cancer is in the ascendant. 23.

Gemini and Sagittarius obey the Head of the Dragon <note>The North Node</note> and the Tail <note>The South Node</note> more than other signs; therefore they cause heavier evils in them than in other signs. 24.

In the births of women, when the ascendant is one of the houses of Venus with Mars existing in them, or one of the houses of Mars with Venus existing in them: the woman will be unchaste. The same will be if the ascendant has Capricorn. M. 25.

The Sun receives the virtues of the planets when he is joined with them in the ascendant or in the house of the midheaven; the Moon does this same thing at night similarly when she is joined with the aforesaid in the same places. 26.

Jupiter dissolves the malice of Saturn: just as Venus does that of Mars. 27.

When there is an inquiry for a woman, simply take the signification from Venus. Specifically, however, from the 7th house. And if for an enemy, absolutely from the 12th. Specifically, however, from the 7th. 28.

In the departure of anyone to battle, but especially of kings, let the ascendant be some house of the superior planets or of the Sun: and let both it and its lord be strong. But let the lord of the 7th be weak and hindered by evils. 29.

The Moon joined to Saturn or Jupiter, increased in light and number, will be good for all things. If, however, she is diminished, she will be bad for all things. Understand the total contrary in Venus and Mars. 30.

In loans and deposits, beware lest Jupiter be under the rays <note>Too close to the Sun to be seen</note> or hindered by evils. For if it be so: and not received from the hindrance, no or little restitution will follow. 31.

Benevolent planets going to a configuration of malefic ones, from whatever figure, diminish something of their malice. And through good figures more; through bad ones truly less. But benevolent ones going to a configuration of good ones from a square or opposition diminish something of their goodness; from other aspects, however, nothing. 32.

When Saturn passes from one sign into another, there are in the sky <term>diaco hontes</term> <note>Likely a corruption of 'dracontes' or meteors</note> which the Arabs call <term>assub</term> <note>original: "assub"; likely referring to 'shihab' or shooting stars</note>: or certain high signs of the nature of fire. 33.

The temperance of the air happens from the conjunction of Jupiter and the Sun, especially when the conjunction itself is in airy signs. From the association of Saturn and the Sun or Jupiter, there is cold: And from the conjunction of the Sun and Mars in a bicorporeal sign and in the time of spring, there is darkness of the air and infirmities frequently happen. 34.

In the summer time when the Sun enters the term of Mars, there is heat: In winter, dryness and a scarcity of rains will be present. 35.

Make in births and questions of men the <term>hyleg</term> <note>The giver of life</note> and <term>alcochoden</term> <note>The giver of years</note> and their directions, and especially in questions of kings and magnates. For through them their accidents are known, whether they be good or bad. 36.

When the ascendant is of good state and its lord is evil: it indicates health of the body but anxiety and sadness of the soul. If, however, it be the other way around, the definition is the other way around. 37.

You shall always note the configuration of the stars not by signs but by rays. 38.

Use the Moon in curing the eyes when she is increased in light and free from the aspect of the malefic planets. 39.

If the Part of Fortune be with a malefic in the 4th, or 9th, or 12th: the removal of the benevolent planets indicates death to the sick. 40.

Good or evil things will be durable when the significator is stationary and in an angle. Truly, they are easily changeable when the same is retrograde and in the cadent houses of the angles. 41.

The lord of the 2nd house has the same power in hindering as the lord of the 8th. Similarly, the lord of the 6th the same as the lord of the 12th. 42.

With Mars occidental in Cancer, not beholding Saturn nor Jupiter nor Venus nor the Sun, he will be a <term>phlebotomator</term> <note>A bloodletter or surgeon</note>. In the same way, with Mars existing in Capricorn, he will be a corrupter of men and a lover of the shedding of blood. 43.

It happens that a skilled Astrologer errs when he is ignorant of the significator of the one for whom he chooses. 44.

Saturn elevated above Venus by a square makes the natives unchaste and in women impatient: But if Venus be elevated above Saturn, she will make them easy and prone toward women. 45.

If in a birth Mercury is in the ascendant, oriental and swift, then the native may be eloquent: and learned in the liberal sciences: the same will be also if he is in Sagittarius in his own terms in such a manner. 46.

The first of the angles is the <term>horoscopus</term> <note>The Ascendant</note>. The second is the midheaven. The third is the west: and the fourth is the angle of the earth <note>The Imum Coeli</note>. Of the remaining places, the first is the 11th, then the 2nd, after that the 5th, then the 9th, after that the 3rd, after which the 8th. The 6th and the 2nd are the worst. 47.

Unless a benevolent planet prohibits it, whatever Mars gives, he takes back. 48.

You shall adapt your petitions to the persons to whom you will supplicate. 49.

When the horoscope or a planet is found in the 30th degree of a sign, its signification is in the following sign: if, however, in the 29th, it will be in that sign itself. For its power lasts in 3 degrees: namely, in that in which it is, and in the preceding and succeeding ones. 50.

<summary>This page lists fifty astrological aphorisms (numbered 2-50) detailing the influences of planets like Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn on human character, health, and the success of various undertakings. It provides specific rules for interpreting birth charts and answering horary questions regarding family, travel, and social status.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, aphorisms, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Moon, houses, aspects, medical astrology, hyleg, alcochoden, horoscopus, phlebotomy</keywords>

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues a series of astrological aphorisms, likely from a Renaissance edition of the "Centiloquium" (a collection of 100 sayings often attributed to Ptolemy). The text focuses on natal charts, medical astrology, and mundane (world) predictions.</meta>

One must consider future events from the conjunctions <note>The alignment of two planets</note> of the planets, but the present or the past from their separations. 51.

If Jupiter is in Cancer, far from the ascendant <note>The degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon</note> and not hindered by any other planet: the person born will indeed be rational and very skilled in science; however, he will love a solitary life and will not receive praise for his knowledge. 52.

Many inconveniences occur in the world when an eclipse of both luminaries <note>The Sun and the Moon</note> happens in one month: and especially in those places where their significance is specific. 53.

If the Moon is in the "combust way" <note>original: "via combusta"; a specific unlucky stretch of the zodiac, usually late Libra to early Scorpio</note> at the time a journey begins, the traveler will fall ill during his pilgrimage or suffer some heavy troubles. 54.

It is necessary to consider the time in the directions of both the wandering stars <note>planets</note> and the fixed stars. 55.

The paternal inheritance passes to the son when Saturn is fortunate and looks upon the Lord of the Ascendant with favor—even more so if he is the Lord of the 4th House <note>The house associated with fathers and ancestry</note>. 56.

When the benefics <note>kindly planets, like Jupiter or Venus</note> are in signs where they have no dignity <note>astrological standing or "rank"</note>, their kindness is poured away. 57.

If Mars is the *Almuten* <note>The planet with the most calculated strength</note> in a birth chart and is not joined to the benefics, it signifies that the person born will suffer from fire. 58.

A benefic planet grants immense happiness when it is received in its own house. A malefic <note>harmful planet, like Saturn or Mars</note>, however, restrains itself from much malice when it is received in such a way. 59.

The person born will be sickly and weak when Mars is elevated above Saturn. But if Saturn is above Mars, he will be stronger and stout. 60.

If the "Part of Marriage" for a man falls in the obedient signs, but for the woman in the commanding signs: the woman will indeed rule the man, and the man will obey the woman. If it is the other way around, judge it the other way around. 61.

If the Lords of the Triplicity <note>Planets ruling a group of three zodiac signs of the same element</note> are joined to each other by conjunction of light out of favor: they signify prosperity and liberation from sorrows according to the first, second, and third [lords]. 62.

When Mercury is in Pisces in the "pitted degrees" <note>original: "gradibus puteosis"; specific degrees thought to weaken a planet's power</note>, it makes those born foolish or mute. Jupiter, however, in the houses of Mars and in the pitted degrees, makes people filthy, needy, and harmed by military persons. In the houses of Saturn, and especially in Capricorn and in the pitted degrees, it makes them rigid and hateful to everyone. 63.

Mercury takes on the nature of Mars when existing in his houses; if he is configured to him while falling from an angle <note>positioned in a weaker area of the chart</note>, the person born will be a lover of hunting and will love to play with dice. If he is not falling, he will be warlike and a soldier. 64.

Planets existing under the [Sun's] rays are unfortunate when they are within 12 degrees of the Sun, unless they are in the same degree with it. But when they have passed 12 degrees and are rising in the east, they are strong. 65.

The Head <note>The North Node of the Moon</note> works terrible evils with the malefics, for it increases their malice. But it works many good things with the benefics, for through it their kindness is increased. The Tail <note>The South Node</note>, however, is opposed to the Head in its significations, just as in misfortunes. 66.

If anyone has Mercury in the 6th house of his birth chart, he will be converted from his faith to another. And he who has the "Part of Fortune" hindered will not be firm in his faith. 67.

The first sign has dominion in significations when the meaning of a matter is made through two signs. 68.

The beginning of every matter is taken from the Moon; but every end is taken from the Lord of her house. 69.

If Jupiter, in the revolution of the years of the world <note>A chart for the start of the year</note>, is in his own house or exaltation, rising in an angle and free from the malefics, it signifies a scarcity of grain. 70.

There is fear for the sick when the Moon and the Lord of the Ascendant are hindered by the Lord of the 8th house <note>The house of death</note>. 71.

In no way should you begin a quarrel, contention, or lawsuit when the Moon is in a bad state. For if it happens in such a way, you will be defeated without a doubt. 72.

Every rebel beginning to rebel at the start of the year will obtain dominion without difficulty. 73.

When the Moon is in the "ruminating signs" <note>Signs like Aries, Taurus, or Capricorn, associated with animals that chew cud</note> or joined to a retrograde planet, it is not good to use purges. For they do not bring about vomiting or other [intended] effects. 74.

<column-break/>

Eastern planets giving good or evil things give them quickly; Western planets give them slowly. 75.

The degree of the conjunction of the luminaries is the midpoint of the duration of an eclipse. 76.

In the revolution of the years of the world, there will be many difficulties and battles when Jupiter and Saturn are in their exaltations. 77.

Be suspicious and anxious when a benefic is with a malefic; do not trust greatly that the malice of the evil one is entirely turned away. 78.

The twelve signs depend on the ascendant: and the ascendant indeed signifies the state of the body, but its Lord signifies the state of the soul. Therefore, strive in every way that the ascendant and its Lord are not hindered. 79.

In fixed signs, planets signify a durable thing; in double-bodied signs <note>Mutable signs like Gemini or Pisces</note>, a doubtful thing; in movable signs, a thing changeable for either good or evil. 80.

Use the Moon for secret matters not when she is entering under the [Sun's] rays, but when she is leaving them. 81.

To cut or put on new clothes when the Moon is in a fixed sign—and especially in Leo—is horrible and dangerous; even more so when she is in conjunction or opposition to the Sun, or hindered by malefics. 82.

The Moon has great power in questions <note>Horary astrology</note> unless the ascendant is Leo, Sagittarius, or Aquarius; for then some of these take away her significance. And especially when she is in Leo or Aquarius. 83.

Saturn is still under the rays while he exists within a distance of 15 degrees between himself and the Sun; understand the same regarding Jupiter. 84.

The Moon is completely rejected for marriages when she is in Cancer or Virgo, except in the marriages of widows. 85.

A malefic, when he is eastern in his own house or exaltation, is better than a retrograde and hindered benefic. 86.

There will be an impediment regarding that part of the body signified by the sign that was hindered at the time of birth. 87.

Immense prosperities occur when the Lords of the Triplicity of the luminary which has authority are in the angles, or in the houses following the angles, in their own places and far from the aspect of malefics. And if the Lord of the Ascendant is in a good state, the good will be even greater. 88.

Planets do the same through a trine aspect <note>120 degrees</note> as they do through a sextile <note>60 degrees</note>; however, the sextile aspect brings less in good or evil than the trine. 89.

Saturn brings evils with slowness, but Mars brings them suddenly; and therefore Mars is considered worse than Saturn in doing harm. 90.

It is called a "Great Conjunction" when the three superior planets <note>Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars</note> join in one of the royal signs; and they create very powerful kingdoms when they are aspected by the Sun. 91.

A hidden word that is sought after is revealed immediately when the Moon and the planet to which she applies are in "voiced" signs <note>Signs associated with human speech, like Gemini or Virgo</note>, and in the 1st, 10th, or 3rd houses, or in their oppositions. 92.

A malefic increases his malice in the 8th house; a benefic, however, shows neither good nor evil there. 93.

Good or evil will never be perfected unless the benefic or malefic planets in the birth chart or in the revolution look upon the Moon by a square aspect <note>90 degrees</note>. 94.

If Mercury is hindered in the 6th house, the person born will die in prison; if Saturn is in the 12th, he will be shut in; and if Venus, he will die by a fall. 95.

One must fear at the beginning of a sickness when the Sun by day and the Moon by night are hindered. 96.

The significations of the stars always vary when their latitudes vary in their configurations to one another. 97.

The Moon in the 4th, 7th, 9th, or 12th houses indicates that the matter questioned has already been asked. The same is indicated if she has separated from Mercury. But if the ascendant is a double-bodied sign and the Moon is in a double-bodied sign, the same matter will be sought again. 98.

In its own house or exaltation, if a planet is malefic, although with slowness, it nevertheless shows a good end. But if the same is hindered in the ascendant, even if in its own house or exaltation, it nevertheless brings an impediment and an evil end. 99.

<summary>This page lists a series of astrological aphorisms (numbered 51-99) providing rules for interpreting birth charts, medical outcomes, and political events based on planetary positions and aspects.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, aphorisms, Ptolemy, Centiloquium, natal charts, planets, zodiac signs, medical astrology, mundane astrology</keywords>

--- Page 57 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "Centiloquium" (Hundred Aphorisms) of Hermes Trismegistus and begins the alphabetical index (Register) for the propositions of Almansor, a common pairing in medieval and Renaissance astrological compendiums.</meta>

The end of every beginning and of all doubtful matters is determined by the outcome of its significations: namely, through the 4th house <note>The 4th house in astrology relates to the "end of things" and ancestral matters</note>, its lord, and by any planet that happens to be in the same. Likewise, by the luminary <note>The Sun or Moon</note> which holds authority, and its lord; and by the Moon to which that luminary is joined, and her lord. 100.

Use these and the other things I have handed down to you, most dear one; and by using them with sobriety, you shall never err, with God's help. Amen.

## Here ends the Book of the Hundred Aphorisms of Hermes.

## The Judgments or Propositions of Almansor. Here begin the chapters on the stars presented to the Great King of the Saracens by Almansor the Astrologer: and translated by Plato of Tivoli.

You have requested of <unclear>me</unclear>, O King, a small compendium of these leaves so that I might satisfy your wishes; I have by no means refused to undergo the labor. I ask that you receive it with a calm mind. To speak of the arrangement of the signs, the beginning is made from Aries: 1. that is, one is diurnal, the other indeed nocturnal; one masculine, the other feminine; one light, the other heavy. 1.

The exaltation of each of the seven planets is said to be in that place in which it substantially suffers the opposite from on high: just as the Sun is in Aries, which is the fall of Saturn; for the Sun has brightness, while Saturn has darkness. And as Jupiter is in Cancer, in which Mars falls; one of whom desires justice, while the other signifies injustice. And so Mercury is in Virgo, which is the fall of Venus; for one signifies science and philosophy, while the other signifies songs, joys, and whatever is flavorful to the body. 2.

<sig>B 2</sig>

<summary>This page concludes an alphabetical index of astrological aphorisms and begins the "Judgments of Almansor," a classic medieval astrological text translated from Arabic into Latin by Plato of Tivoli. The opening chapters define the basic qualities of the Zodiac signs and the concept of planetary "exaltations" based on opposing elemental or moral qualities.</summary>
<keywords>Almansor, Astrology, Plato of Tivoli, Zodiac signs, Exaltation, Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Venus, Mercury, Medieval Science</keywords>

--- Page 59 ---
<meta>This page continues a series of astrological and medical aphorisms, likely from a medieval or Renaissance Latin translation of an Arabic source (possibly Almansor or a similar authority). The text is organized into numbered points.</meta>

The meaning of the parts of the circle regarding great matters is as follows: just as the sign of a fiery part signifies fire, so the Earthly signifies earth, the Airy signifies air, and the Watery signifies water. **3.**

Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius are the hot and dry **triplicity** <note>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element.</note>: bitter, fiery, diurnal, masculine; and it signifies red choler <note>One of the four bodily humors, associated with anger and fire.</note>.
Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn: the earthly triplicity: cold and dry, nocturnal, feminine; and it signifies black choler.
Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius: the triplicity is airy: hot, moist, sanguine, sweet, and diurnal.
Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces: the watery triplicity is cold, moist, phlegmatic, tasteless, and nocturnal. **4.**

The Fortunes <note>Benefic planets, usually Jupiter and Venus.</note> are generous and faithful. The Infortunes <note>Malefic planets, usually Saturn and Mars.</note> truly are greedy and unfaithful. **5.**

The Fortunes will give nothing to anyone without labor. But the Infortunes, whatever they give, they will shorten and take away. **6.**

When two Infortunes are joined together, a perfect fortune is made from them, just as from the conjunction of two Fortunes; and this is <term>according to</term> <note>original: "sm" (secundum)</note> the sayings of Ptolemy. **7.**

Of all the aspects of the planets, the Trine and Sextile are the best; but the Square and Opposition are the worst. **8.**

The Trine and Sextile aspect of the Infortunes does no good, just as the Square and Opposition of the Fortunes does no harm. **9.**

Out of many significations of life, in the <term>hyleg</term> <note>The "giver of life," a specific point in a birth chart used to determine longevity.</note> it signifies a great and long life, a pure intellect, and the greatest vigor. **10.**

Whosoever has Mercury in the root of their nativity <note>The birth chart.</note> in the house of Mars: they will be prone to evil suspicion and hasty in their business. **11.**

Whosoever has Mercury in the root of their nativity in the house of Saturn: they will be of great intellect and long-reaching thought, a wise man and a philosopher. **12.**

He whose lord of nativity is Jupiter shall never be poor or destitute. **13.**

Whosoever has the Infortunes in the Ascendant shall suffer a foul mark upon the face. **14.**

The eyes of one whose both lights <note>The Sun and the Moon.</note> were afflicted in their nativity will not be able to escape a heavy impediment. **15.**

He in whose nativity the Moon goes toward Mercury shall not lose his senses. **16.**

When the Fortunes preside over the nativity, the newborn will be generous and lovable; but if the Infortunes preside, he will be greedy and abhorrent. **17.**

He whom Saturn presided over at birth will be filthy, and he whom Mars presided over will be foul-smelling. **18.**

In whosoever's nativity the Infortunes are in the eighth house, they shall die an evil death. But if the Fortunes are there, they shall pass away in their own bed. **19.**

No one shall gather money or hoard treasure except him whose lord of the Ascendant and lord of the fourth house are the same planet. Likewise, no one shall gain money or riches, nor live splendidly, except him whose lord of the Ascendant and lord of the tenth house are the same. **20.**

There can be no affection between two people unless the lights in the root of their nativities are exchanged—that is, so that one is in the place of the other in the nativity of their companion. Truly, those who are born in opposition to these are hateful to one another, being in opposite signs or squares, or with the lords of those signs being in this manner, or with the lights beholding each other in that way. **21.**

The lord of the fifth circle, namely Venus, signifies soakings and rains; just as the lord of the seventh, namely Mercury, signifies journeys, changes, and pilgrimages. **22.**

He who approaches a King while the Moon is in Aquarius will neither be received nor will the King care for him in any way; but if he approaches him while she is in Pisces, the King will turn his face away from him. **23.**

Bloodletting will not be good when the Moon is in Gemini, just as it will not be suitable to use cupping glasses when the Moon is in Taurus. **24.**

The best signs for taking laxatives and purgatives are the watery ones, but the best of them is Scorpio. Cancer, however, is considered the worst. **25.**

The best sign for partnerships is Leo; the worst is Aries. **26.**
Fixed stars give grand gifts and raise one up from poverty to

<column-break/>

a height that the planets do not. **27.**

He will be a perfect physician in whose chart Mars and Venus are in the sixth house. He will be a good pleader <note>A lawyer or advocate.</note> in whose chart Mercury is retrograde with Venus in the same sign. **28.**

The lord of the fifth circle, namely Venus, when she is in the first degree of Cancer, causes rains. **29.**

When the heavy planets are western of the Sun, they give integrity toward the end of life, and vice versa. **30.**

He who buys anything while the Moon is from the head of Cancer until the end of Sagittarius will buy dearly and sell cheaply. It is the opposite when it is from the beginning of Capricorn until the end of Gemini. **31.**

Circumcision is not good when the Moon is in Scorpio, just as vomiting is not good in Leo. **32.**

Venus attributes the nature of the sign she is in to the person born. For when she is in Leo, she gives love; in Taurus, <note>unclear: causatus</note>; and in Scorpio, sexual intercourse. The reason is because Leo holds the heart, Taurus the throat and neck, and Scorpio the genitals. **33.**

Whosoever has Mercury in the twelfth house will be wise and a great philosopher. **34.**

When the Moon is afflicted in the root of the nativity, and all the lords of the triplicities—namely the <term>hyleg</term>—have fallen, and especially if the first lord of the triplicity of the Ascendant is in some angle with an Infortune, it indicates a short life for the newborn. **35.**

The lord of the fifth circle, namely Venus, dissolves what the lord of the third binds, which is Mars. **36.**

But the lord of the second, namely Jupiter, dissolves what the lord of the first binds, namely Saturn; and this significance is over great matters. **36.** <note>The numbering repeats in the original.</note>

When all the lords of the triplicities have fallen, and one of the fixed stars of the first or second exaltation—which is of the nature of the lords of the triplicities—is in one of the angles, then nourishment will be completed and will surpass it. **37.**

That which falls upon the Moon from the malice of Mars when she is increasing in light is equal to that which falls upon her from the malice of Saturn when she is diminished in light. This chapter signifies great things. Nor will a heretic be known except by the lord of the second circle. And the lord of the fourth circle signifies kings. **38.**

In the elections <note>The choosing of astrologically favorable times to act.</note> of kings, the signs of the higher planets are commended; just as in the elections of the powerless, the signs of the lower planets are commended. **39.**

The things that indicate a higher fortune are Jupiter, the Part of Fortune, the Part of Dominion, also the second house and whoever is in it and its lord; also the lord of the triplicity of the light which has authority, as well as the lord of the tenth and whoever is in it. **40.**

When the significator is between the Ascendant and the 10th house, it will be days and hours; when between the 10th and the 7th, it will be weeks and months; but when it is between the 7th and the 4th, it will be years. **41.**

The lord of the first circle, namely Saturn, when he is in fixed signs, causes mortality and dearness <note>High prices/famine.</note>; this the wise have proven by experience. **42.**

When you wish to choose a time for someone and you cannot delay, if the Moon is afflicted, place the afflicting planet as the lord of the Ascendant. **43.**

The best <unclear>position</unclear> <note>original: "scrisal"</note> is in longitude and latitude; it strengthens the good nature of a Fortune and makes a bad nature vile; the Infortunes, however, do the opposite. **44.**

Whichever of the planets is superior, its action will not cease until its contrary is in the same place. **45.**

When you are unable to delay an election, place the Ascendant and its lord in safety; also place the names of the Fortunes in an angle, and what is better, in the 10th house. **46.**

Mars does no harm in travels by water, just as Saturn does no harm in travels by land. In journeys, fixed signs are disparaged, but movable signs are commended. **47.**

When an Infortune looks upon the significator and is retrograde and falling, and also in a place where it is peregrine—that is, in none of its dignities—and in a sign contrary to its nature, it brings an evil upon the significator that no one can cure except God alone. **48.**

When Mars approaches the earth, he is pacified with Ju... <note>Text cuts off; likely "Jupiter".</note>

<meta>This page continues the numbered astrological aphorisms, likely from a version of the Centiloquium (a collection of 100 astrological rules). The previous page would have contained aphorisms 1 through 48.</meta>

# Page 27

ve. Saturn also will be pacified here with Venus, and the Moon when she has approached the Earth: and there is an ascension of two parts in one part. <margin>49.</margin>

The Moon accords with the Sun when she has been increased in light and in number <note>referring to the waxing moon and its numerical degrees</note>. Every luminous star also accords with her. <margin>50.</margin>

When Mars weakens, Venus is strengthened: likewise, Jupiter diminishes the malice of the unfortunate planets <note>the malefics, Saturn and Mars</note> when Saturn is weak, provided he is in some of his own dignities, and vice versa. <margin>51.</margin>

When a fortune <note>benefic planet</note> receives an misfortune <note>malefic planet</note>, it will not hinder her. This is especially true when it does not aspect her from the fourth or opposite position, nor is in the same sign. The greatest hindrance of the planets is when they are in foreign places <note>peregrine, or signs where they have no essential dignity</note>. <margin>52.</margin>

Every planet, when it is joined to the Sun in the same point, will go swiftly: when it approaches him, it will go slowly. <margin>53.</margin>

The strongest testimony is the *Ictisal* <term>Ictisal: an Arabic astrological term for the "application" or joining of two planets</term> of the Moon to a planet that is in the midheaven in the ascendant. <margin>54.</margin>

Cancer is the significator over very mobile waters; Scorpio, however, over the running waters of streams; Pisces also over immobile waters, namely, such as pools and wells. <margin>55.</margin>

Every matter that is done hastily and destroyed hastily, and which is repeated, is under the signification of Mars. <margin>56.</margin>

Concerning those things which are done in haste and suddenly destroyed, yet remain destroyed for a long time: this is of the nature of Saturn and Mars. <margin>57.</margin>

Whoever is of royal lineage: if in the root of his birth <note>his natal chart</note> there were two Suns <note>likely referring to the Sun and a planet acting as a second Sun by dignity</note>, he will be contrary to kings and will be separated from them in his works. <margin>58.</margin>

For whomever the Moon is in Taurus at the hour of birth in the minute of the ascension, and the Sun is in Leo in the ascendant in the minute of the ascension, he shall arrive at great exaltation. <margin>59.</margin>

Those things which happen in this world are known and investigated from the great strength of the superior significator and from his elevation: and if that is not present, it is sought from the planet to which all other planets are directed. <margin>60.</margin>

If any of the planets signified something in the root of the birth: when the division and the lord <note>the chronocrator or time-lord</note> reach it, its signification will appear, whether it be good or evil. <margin>61.</margin>

When the lord of the 10th house is retrograde from the *Ictisal* <note>application</note> of the lord of the ascendant: the native shall receive one <note>gift/benefit</note> from his lord. When the lord of the ascendant and the Moon are in ascension, and two fortunes <note>benefics</note> likewise aspect each other, the native will be very strong and powerful: and no one shall bypass his command. <margin>62.</margin>

From the degree of the ascension, the accidents of the body are known; from the degree of the Part of Fortune, the state of substance <note>wealth</note>. From the degree of the Moon, the state of the body and soul is perceived; from the degree of the Sun, however, his health; but from the degree of the midheaven, his masteries and works are distinguished: give one year to each degree. <margin>63.</margin>

He shall be fortunate and of good being, and also powerful, whose year of revolution <note>solar return</note> is similar to the root <note>natal chart</note>, and whose circle is in the same likeness as it was in the year of the root itself. <margin>64.</margin>

The daily state of the native is taken from the direction; otherwise from the motion of the lord; otherwise, the day-to-day state of the native is taken from the motion of the lord of the sign of the profection of the year in the set terms, and the lord of the sign of the *alinthie* <term>alinthie: likely a corruption of the Arabic 'al-intihā' meaning the profection or year-lord</term> of the year to the set terms. <margin>65.</margin>

When Saturn ascends in the higher part of his circle (that is, in his apogee) and the Moon is joined to the Sun at the end of the month and she is with him: it shall signify an increase of the thing which is of the nature of the sign in which he resides. <margin>66.</margin>

When a planet is in the very minute of the eastern degree <note>the Ascendant</note> and the Moon is joined to it in the same place, the act of that planet's nature will appear, whether it be good or evil. <margin>67.</margin>

When any planet rules any year among the years of the world, being in the very degree of its exaltation, its signification will appear, and the King will be elevated in that region and in the climate which is signified by it. <margin>68.</margin>

The hindrance of Mercury by Saturn ought to impede the tongue of the native: and it is worse if they are joined bodily <note>by conjunction</note>. <margin>69.</margin>

How great will be the fear for the sick person when the luminaries <note>Sun and Moon</note> are beneath the earth at the hour of the interrogation <note>the time the horary question is asked</note>. <margin>70.</margin>

---

When both luminaries are in the same degree of their exaltation in their own dominion, free from misfortunes, the native shall be King of the whole world: and his seed shall inherit his lands and shall hold them for a long time. <margin>71.</margin>

When the ascendant of any birth is the midheaven of the world, and it is a moveable sign—namely Aries or Cancer—and that degree which is upon the line of the midheaven is the same degree in which the Sun or Jupiter is exalted, his name shall spread through the universal earth and his fame shall be made known. Nor are those things to be ignored which can be adapted from them: namely the ascendant, the Part of Fortune, the luminaries, the sign of their conjunction and their lords, the sign of the lord of the hour, and also the place of the thing sought and its lord. <margin>72.</margin>

From the lord of the exaltation and the divider, and also the lord of the other divided rays, and from the lord of the year, and from the movement of the planets in their places and their aspects, the accidents of the World are known in the Revolutions of the Years. <margin>73.</margin>

When the lord of the 10th house is in the 8th: it is feared lest the mother of the native die from that birth. <margin>74.</margin>

When the lord of the 4th house is hindered by the lord of the ascendant, there shall be fear for the part of the native <note>likely his inheritance or father</note>. <margin>75.</margin>

Nothing profits a combatant when the lord of the ascendant is an misfortune, and retrograde or under the rays <note>combust by the Sun</note>; likewise if he is with the 7th house or its lord, he who began to fight first shall prevail in the battle. <margin>76.</margin>

An misfortune heats and hinders its own place; the Sun, however, heats and does not hinder. <margin>77.</margin>

Beware lest the King go out to battle when the lord of the ascendant is going toward the lord of the 7th. <margin>78.</margin>

When Mars is with the Sun in northern signs, there will be the greatest heat; likewise also when the Sun goes to Saturn in southern signs, there will be the greatest cold, and vice versa. <margin>79.</margin>

When misfortunes reach a convenient place, they will not harm if their signification was not in the root of the birth; and likewise, fortunes will not profit when there is no signification from them in the root. <margin>80.</margin>

The hindrances that are to come in the years of the *alinthie* <note>profection</note> happen when the year of the world reaches the bodies of the evils <note>malefics</note>; give one year to each sign. <margin>81.</margin>

The greater misfortune of Saturn is when he is in feminine signs; but of Mars when he is in masculine signs. <margin>82.</margin>

No one ought to attack a city whose lord is the lord of the ascendant of the year of the world. <margin>83.</margin>

It is necessary in every beginning to adapt the circle <note>the chart</note> to the nature of that which one is about to begin. <margin>84.</margin>

Let the *Ictisal* <note>application</note> of the Moon to Saturn from the houses of Venus not be consumed <note>avoided</note>; nor to Jupiter from the houses of Saturn and Mercury; nor to the Sun from the houses of Saturn. <margin>85.</margin>

Among the greater fortunes is that the planets be diurnal, Eastern from the Sun in masculine signs; but nocturnal planets should be Western from the Moon. <margin>86.</margin>

When the lords of the triplicity of the luminary of the day are diurnal and eastern, and the lords of the triplicity of the night are western and increased in light, it will be a greater sign of fortune and happiness. <margin>87.</margin>

The eastern point <note>Ascendant</note> signifies children, youths, and the beginnings of everything; the midheaven signifies Kings, lords of the law, judges, and also lords of battles; the seventh signifies old men and the deceased, also women and the smallest things and everything beautiful; the angle of the earth <note>Imum Coeli</note> signifies fathers and lands, also the place in which the infant was born; it also demonstrates the delay of death and sepulchers. <margin>88.</margin>

The planets that give great riches are three: namely Jupiter, the Sun, and Mercury. <margin>89.</margin>

Every beginning by one who possesses many riches is to be noted in all mid-points in which the conjunction or prevention <note>syzygy</note> is hindered; let him therefore beware of this. <margin>90.</margin>

A universal question is likened to a birth: revolve its years, therefore, and judge upon them. <margin>91.</margin>

<margin>92.</margin>

<summary>This page lists a series of astrological aphorisms (49-91) detailing the influences of planets on human life, royal success, weather, and war. It emphasizes the importance of the natal chart ("the root") in determining whether future planetary transits will have a significant effect.</summary>

--- Page 61 ---
<meta>The text consists of astrological aphorisms, likely from a medieval or Renaissance collection such as the Centiloquium. It focuses on the influence of planets (Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, etc.) on human life, kingship, and health.</meta>

Saturn rejoices in Aquarius, just as Jupiter rejoices in Pisces.
Mars also rejoices in Scorpio, just as Venus rejoices in
Libra, and Mercury in Virgo. 93.

Just as the Dragon's Head <note>original: "Beuzahar", the North Node of the Moon</note> adds to the nature of every planet that
is with it, so the Tail <note>the South Node</note> diminishes it, according to the nature of whichever planet
is with it. 94.

The strongest indication regarding the father's existence is the firstborn,
and those who follow will provide some part of the meaning. 95.

The "Algebuihar" <note>a calculation for the giver of life</note> is stronger in the cause of life. However, the "Alcocoden" <note>the giver of years</note> is stronger
in other matters. Truly, the Lord of Rays is stronger
than the Algebuihar, and strong things will be equally strong. 96.

When one dies before a single day and a single
night have passed, the newborn will be like a miscarriage and will have no
significance in anything. 97.

Kings whose business is done quickly, and who avenge themselves quickly,
who also quickly perfect what they desire, are those whose
Ascendant <note>the degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon</note> at the beginning of their reign was a fiery sign,
and likewise the Midheaven <note>original: "medium celi", the highest point of the chart</note>; or if one was fiery and the
other was airy. 98.

When the Lord of the Ascendant is in a good place within the terms
of the Fortunes <note>Jupiter and Venus</note>, and the Lord of the term is well-connected <note>original: "colligatus"</note>,
it will grant integrity, kingship, and every good thing. 99.

When it is in the Midheaven, it signifies that it will be directed through the ascension
of the direct circle; but in the Ascendant, through the ascensions
of the region. In that which is between the two, through
both. Al-Kindi <note>original: "alchindus", a famous 9th-century Arab philosopher and astrologer</note> related this chapter. 100.

The cause of good fortune and prosperity is that the Lord of the House
of the Sun, the Lord of the House of the Moon, and the Lord of the Ascendant be
oriental <note>rising before the sun</note>; let them also be in angles <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> and aspect each other from good places,
that is, by trine or sextile <note>harmonious angles of 120 or 60 degrees</note>. 101.

Very powerful is he for whom the Sun is in the Midheaven in a fiery
sign, and the Moon is in Taurus, whose aspect will be on the left. 102.

Those who are very rich and abound in wealth and are of great
repute are those for whom the Lord of the 2nd House is in its exaltation
or its own house, moving toward the Lord of the Ascendant.
Especially so if it is Jupiter. 103.

The life of all living things is according to the degree of the Sun and the Moon,
and this is given by the Most High according to the stars <note>original: "astapham"</note>. 104.

When a Fortune <note>Jupiter or Venus</note> is in the places of Mars, and the significator is toward
it or it toward the significator, it will rise like a Misfortune <note>original: "infortuẽ"</note>. 105.

Turn your eyes away from a figure in which Mars is in an angle,
especially when the Ascendant is Scorpio. 106.

When someone wishes to take something away, if the significator
is looking at Saturn, he will take it away with great difficulty. 107.

If anyone asks for something while the Dragon's Head is in the Midheaven
with Jupiter, and the Moon is going toward him; or if, having separated from him,
she goes to the Lord of the Ascendant; or if the Lord of the
Ascendant goes to him: he will not fail to briefly
obtain the things sought. 108.

It is not good for one wishing to wage war, nor for a king to
make a journey, when the Lord of the Ascendant is in the 8th house,
even if it is in its exaltation. 109.

The Sun is not good in the Ascendant at the hour of war, nor with the Lord
of the Ascendant, unless the Ascendant is Aries or Leo. 110.

In that part where the Sun and Moon are made fortunate, there will be
victory for those inquiring; just as if they are made fortunate from the Ascendant
to the Midheaven, or from the Ascendant to the 4th house, the seeker
will win; if it is in other places, the adversary will overcome. 111.

And the greatest impediment to things is in the
power of the sign in which there is a falling planet, or one that is
retrograde, or in a bad state regarding the Sun. 112.

Signs signify bodies; Planets truly signify those things which move
the bodies; the places of the planets in the circle and their substances,
and their places from the Sun, signify work and destruction. 113.

In whosever Nativity Jupiter is the receiver of the
strengths of all planets, and he himself commends
them and his own to Saturn, and he is received by him, and both are
oriental and the Ascendant is in an angle: the newborn will be great and
powerful in this world, and also of good character; his
name shall also rise in the world. 114.

Beware lest Saturn be with the Lord of the Ascendant or in other
exalted places, because he himself is worse than the Dragon's Tail. 115.

---

There will be clear rumors if, at the hour of the announcement, the angles
are fixed signs, and the Moon and Mercury are in fixed signs; and if
the Moon has also separated from the Fortunes; and also if there is
a Fortune in any of the angles. When you find it so, this
configuration can neither be deceived nor deceive. 116.

The first hour of the day until the completion of three hours
belongs to the blood. The second three to red choler. The third
to black choler; but the last three to phlegm. Similarly
at night. In the same way, it is in the quarters of the lunar
month; the quarters of the year, however, belong to the Sun. 117.

He whose Lord of the Ascendant is to the right of the Sun, and is
elevated above him—that is, he has victory and now
perfects his, so to speak, "orientality"—will be a friend of
kings and powerful men, wealthy also and of great name. 118.

Do not hasten in judging when planets are joined to one another
unless you first consider the nature in which
they are joined: whether it is from their similarity
or not. For if it is from their similarity, you will strengthen their
signification. 119.

When the Lord of the 2nd from the Ascendant, or the 2nd from the Moon, or the 2nd
from the Part of Fortune is surrounded by Fortunes, he will be more
fortunate than any other. 120.

When two Misfortunes are in the degree of the 4th house and in the
same term, the newborn will be unfortunate and very miserable. 121.

When planets are in the angles, those things which are signified
by them will appear, whether they be good or bad. The sign of the
Lord of the 4th circle is the year; but of the 7th circle is the month. 122.

When the angles of the Ascendant are moveable signs and two
Misfortunes are in the angles, the whole life of the newborn will be miserable. 123.

When the Moon is in the angles, she will strengthen all
her signification, and especially if she has any authority
in the Ascendant. 124.

A rapid change cannot be except from Mars, just as
a great delay cannot be except from Saturn. 125.

The status of all good things changes from good to evil,
or from evil to good, when the signs and figures of the planets
which signify them change from ascension to descension
and vice versa. 126.

Jupiter and Saturn change things and turn them; and it will be the
beginning of variation when they change from one triplicity to
another in conjunctions, and from one figure to another. 127.

The two best conjunctions are those of which one is of the
lights <note>the Sun and Moon</note>; the other truly of the two heavy planets.
Masculine planets are those which act; feminine truly are those
acted upon; and similarly also in the signs. 128.

Look at the ascension of the planet and the degree of exaltation,
also the degree of its fall; for these are the things that signify
men and their works. 129.

If you find a masculine significator in the angles and in
a masculine sign, but the significator of the contrary is feminine
and in a feminine sign, he will prevail—that is, he will conquer the
other—whose sign is masculine and in a masculine sign. 130.

Significations are in two ways: substantially and
accidentally; and in both ways they will signify the number of the seven
planets. 131.

When two Misfortunes are joined, and the Moon to Saturn in
latitude, there will be famine and mortality. If truly to Mars,
kings will be changed, and there will be much bloodshed and battles in
the place signified by that sign, and this does not fail. 132.

From the conjunction of Saturn and Jupiter in moveable signs,
the change of the state and the world will be known; and from their
conjunctions in fixed signs again. 133.

When two Fortunes join, and the Moon joins Jupiter in
latitude, there will be justice, and things on earth will be directed. But if to Venus, there will be
alacrity, joy, health of bodies, and prosperity. 134.

When Venus and Mars are in the same point with the Sun in
the place of Venus and her term, the words of the newborn will be
received by men and rejected by no one. 135.

A hermit and like a prophet, and one whose words are received,
is he in whose nativity Jupiter and Venus are in the same
degree with the Sun. 136.

<summary>This page contains a series of astrological aphorisms (numbered 93-136) detailing the influences of planetary placements, conjunctions, and house dignities on human character, health, and political events. It emphasizes the roles of Jupiter and Saturn in world changes and the specific conditions for wealth, power, and prophetic speech.</summary>

--- Page 62 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "Propositions" or "Aphorisms" of Almansor, a collection of astrological rules. It includes the colophon detailing the translation from Arabic to Latin and the printing information for the 1501 Venetian edition.</meta>

<page-num>28</page-num>

Famous kings in this world, whose commands are not scorned, are those for whom Jupiter and the Moon were at the same point of the Ascendant <note>The degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> at their peak. 137.

He who shall have the greatest vigor and great power is he for whom the Sun is in the middle of the sky <note>The Midheaven or 10th House, representing career and public standing</note> with Saturn in a masculine, oriental sign. 138.

When the Moon and other significators <note>Planets representing specific people or themes in a chart</note> are removed from the angles <note>The four most powerful houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th</note>, the matters themselves will not be completed, unless the question concerns a journey and not a high matter. 139.

When there is no *Ictisal* <note>original: "Ictisal" - an Arabic astrological term for the application or joining of two planets by aspect</note> aspect between any of the planets and the significator, but both are in the same circle—whether from parallel circles in the *nadir* <note>The point directly opposite the zenith</note> or *anahat* <note>likely a corruption of an Arabic term for the horizon or a specific celestial circle</note> or in the equinoctial path—it will be better than an *Ictisal* aspect. 140.

Every planet has two signs except for the luminaries <note>The Sun and the Moon</note>, each of which has only one house; because their light is the darkness of Saturn, their houses are placed in opposition. 141.

The Fortunes <note>Benefic planets, usually Jupiter and Venus</note> are faithful and prosperous when they are in their places of exaltation, moving in direct motion, and increasing in light. 142.

In great and exalted riches or the beginnings of things, he praises this: That the luminaries be in the terms <note>Specific subdivisions of zodiac signs</note> of the Fortunes, beholding one another, and that the rich terms be of the same nature as the matter being undertaken. 143.

The matters of the circle which embrace all things, and which are the greatest, are seven: namely, the beginning of the creation of men, and the years of the Great Sun; and they are 1413. <note>This likely refers to a vast astrological cycle or "Great Year"</note>

When, however, some beginning ascends in the term of the ascendant of the root <note>The original birth or founding chart</note> of any undertaking, and finds two minutes of the two heavy planets <note>Saturn and Jupiter</note>: the knowledge of the hour of the year in which they join in the signs; the Ascendant of the revolution of the year of the world; also the Ascendant of the conjunction or prevention <note>The New or Full Moon preceding an event</note> which occurs at the hour of Birth or of the question. 144.

Neither good nor evil shall happen to anyone unless the nature or likeness of the triplicity <note>The grouping of signs by element: Fire, Earth, Air, Water</note> of the signs of the planets, which are the significators of that place, is changed. 145.

When two climates <note>Geographical zones of latitude</note> differ, the aspect of the planets and their rays differ. 146.

When two latitudes differ, the circles of its circular motion differ. In this chapter, however, no one has spoken so perfectly. 147.

The hindrances of the two nodes <note>The North and South Nodes of the Moon, often called the Dragon's Head and Tail</note> are worse for the inferior planets <note>Mercury and Venus</note> than for the superior ones <note>Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</note>. 148.

The figure of the circle in questions bears a great resemblance to the likeness of the soul of the seeker. 149.

If Jupiter should be a light in Aries, in direct motion without a bad aspect from the Fortunes, it will give strength and a kingdom in which no injustice shall be done. 150.

->**The End.**<-

> ¶ The Book of the Propositions of Almansor is perfected, translated with God's help from Arabic into Latin by Plato of Tivoli <note>A famous 12th-century translator active in Spain</note>—whom may God exalt—in the city of Barcelona in the year of the Arabs 530, the 18th day of the month of Dhu al-Qi'dah <note>original: "dullugida"</note>, the Sun being in the 15th degree of Virgo and the Moon in the 15th or 16th degree of Aries.

->**It is finished.**<-
